\displaystyle\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{n}\binom{\alpha_{i,p+1}-|\bm{\alpha}_{i,:p}|}{2\xi_{i}}\frac{(2\xi_{i})!}{\xi_{i}!}\left(\frac{-\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}}{2}\right)^{\xi_{i}}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{w\mathbin{\overset{}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2})}w^{\alpha_{i,p+1}-|\bm{\alpha}_{i,:p}|-2\xi_{i}},

where
\sum_{j=1}^{p}X_{i,j}{\bm{\beta}}_{1,j}z_{i}+\sum_{j=1}^{p}X_{i,j}{\bm{\beta}}_{2,j}(1-z_{i})\mathbin{\overset{}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2})
, both marginally and conditionally on
{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},{\bm{z}}
, and hence is independent of
\prod_{i=1}^{n}\prod_{j=1}^{p}\left({\bm{\beta}}_{1,j}z_{i}+{\bm{\beta}}_{2,j}(1-z_{i})\right)^{\alpha_{i,j}}
(since
{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}
are constrained to have norm
\|{\bm{\beta}}\|_{2}
according to our prior). After switching the sum with the product, combining the known equation for Gaussian moments
\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{w\mathbin{\overset{}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2})}w^{b}=(b-1)!!\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{b}_{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{b\text{ even}\}}
with additional factorial simplifications, and applying Lemma
B.6
, we obtain

\displaystyle\langle\mathsf{L},\tilde{H}_{\bm{\alpha}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\frac{1}{\sigma}}{\sqrt{\frac{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}}{\sigma^{2}}+1}}\right)^{|\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}|}\frac{\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}!}{(\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}-|\bm{\alpha}_{\cdot,:p}|)!}\sum_{0\leq\bm{\xi}\leq\bm{\lfloor\frac{\alpha_{i,p+1}-|\bm{\alpha}_{i,:p}|}{2}\rfloor}}

\displaystyle\cdot\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\binom{\alpha_{i,p+1}-|\bm{\alpha}_{i,:p}|}{2\xi_{i}}\frac{(2\xi_{i})!}{\xi_{i}!}\left(\frac{-\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}}{2}\right)^{\xi_{i}}\right)\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\prod_{j=1}^{p}\left({\bm{\beta}}_{1,j}z_{i}+{\bm{\beta}}_{2,j}(1-z_{i})\right)^{\alpha_{i,j}}\right)

\displaystyle\cdot\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n}(\alpha_{i,p+1}-|\bm{\alpha}_{i,:p}|-2\xi_{i}-1)!!\cdot\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{\alpha_{i,p+1}-|\bm{\alpha}_{i,:p}|-2\xi_{i}}_{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\alpha_{i,p+1}-|\bm{\alpha}_{i,:p}|-2\xi_{i}\text{ even}\}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\frac{1}{\sigma}}{\sqrt{\frac{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}}{\sigma^{2}}+1}}\right)^{|\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}|}\frac{\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}!}{(\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}-|\bm{\alpha}_{\cdot,:p}|)!}\|{\bm{\beta}}\|_{2}^{|\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}|-|\bm{\alpha}_{\cdot,:p}|}

\displaystyle\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{\xi}^{\lfloor\frac{\alpha_{i,p+1}-|\bm{\alpha}_{i,:p}|}{2}\rfloor}\binom{\alpha_{i,p+1}-|\bm{\alpha}_{i,:p}|}{2\xi}\frac{(2\xi)!}{\xi!}\left(\frac{-1}{2}\right)^{\xi}

\displaystyle\cdot(\alpha_{i,p+1}-|\bm{\alpha}_{i,:p}|-2\xi-1)!!\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\{\alpha_{i,p+1}-|\bm{\alpha}_{i,:p}|-2\xi\text{ even}\}}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}}\left(\prod_{i=1}^{n}\prod_{j=1}^{p}\left({\bm{\beta}}_{1,j}z_{i}+{\bm{\beta}}_{2,j}(1-z_{i})\right)^{\alpha_{i,j}}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\frac{1}{\sigma}}{\sqrt{\frac{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}}{\sigma^{2}}+1}}\right)^{|\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}|}\frac{\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}!}{(\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}-|\bm{\alpha}_{\cdot,:p}|)!}\|{\bm{\beta}}\|_{2}^{|\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}|-|\bm{\alpha}_{\cdot,:p}|}

\displaystyle\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\alpha_{i,p+1}-|\bm{\alpha}_{i,:p}|=0\}}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{n}\prod_{j=1}^{p}\left({\bm{\beta}}_{1,j}z_{i}+{\bm{\beta}}_{2,j}(1-z_{i})\right)^{\alpha_{i,j}}\right]

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\frac{1}{\sigma}}{\sqrt{\frac{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}}{\sigma^{2}}+1}}\right)^{|\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}|}\|{\bm{\beta}}\|_{2}^{|\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}|-|\bm{\alpha}_{\cdot,:p}|}\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}!\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\alpha_{i,p+1}-|\bm{\alpha}_{\cdot,:p}|=0\}}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{n}\prod_{j=1}^{p}\left({\bm{\beta}}_{1,j}z_{i}+{\bm{\beta}}_{2,j}(1-z_{i})\right)^{\alpha_{i,j}}\right],

which leads to the desired result.
∎

Lemma B.9
.

Let
({\bm{\beta}}^{(1)}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)}_{2})
and
({\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}_{2})
be two independent copies of signals sampled from
\mathcal{P}_{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|_{2}}(\mathcal{D})
, and likewise for
{\bm{z}}^{(1)}
and
{\bm{z}}^{(2)}
sampled entrywise from
\text{Bernoulli}(\phi)
. We then have

\displaystyle\chi^{2}_{\leq D}({\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})\|{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}})\otimes{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{y}}))+1=\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}({\bm{\beta}}_{1}^{(1)},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}^{(1)}),({\bm{\beta}}_{1}^{(2)},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}^{(2)})\mathbin{\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{P}\\
z^{(1)},z^{(2)}\mathbin{\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\text{Ber}(\phi)\end{subarray}}\,\sum_{\frac{d}{2}=0}^{\lfloor\frac{D}{2}\rfloor}\left(\frac{1}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 35.00005pt\cdot\sum_{|\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}|=\frac{d}{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\langle{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)}_{1}z^{(1)}_{i}+{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)}_{2}(1-z^{(1)}_{i}),{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}_{1}z^{(2)}_{i}+{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}_{2}(1-z^{(2)}_{i})\rangle^{\alpha_{i,p+1}}.

Proof.

We begin the proof by applying Lemma
B.8
to obtain:

\displaystyle\chi^{2}_{\leq D}({\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})\|{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}})\otimes{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{y}}))+1

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq|\bm{\alpha}|\leq D}\frac{1}{\bm{\alpha}!}{\langle\mathsf{L},\tilde{H}_{\bm{\alpha}}\rangle}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq|\bm{\alpha}|\leq D}\frac{1}{\bm{\alpha}!}\left(\frac{\frac{1}{\sigma}}{\sqrt{\frac{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}}{\sigma^{2}}+1}}\right)^{2|\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}|}\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2|\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}|-2|\bm{\alpha}_{\cdot,:p}|}_{2}(\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}!)^{2}

\displaystyle\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\alpha_{i,p+1}-|\bm{\alpha}_{\cdot,:p}|=0\}}\left(\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\prod_{j=1}^{p}\left({\bm{\beta}}_{1,j}z_{i}+{\bm{\beta}}_{2,j}(1-z_{i})\right)^{\alpha_{i,j}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{d=0}^{D}\sum_{h=0}^{d}\sum_{|\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}|=h}\sum_{|\bm{\alpha}_{\cdot,:p}|=d-h}\frac{1}{\bm{\alpha}!}\left(\frac{\frac{1}{\sigma}}{\sqrt{\frac{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}}{\sigma^{2}}+1}}\right)^{2|\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}|}\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2|\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}|-2|\bm{\alpha}_{\cdot,:p}|}_{2}(\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}!)^{2}

\displaystyle\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\alpha_{i,p+1}-|\bm{\alpha}_{\cdot,:p}|=0\}}\left(\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\prod_{j=1}^{p}\left({\bm{\beta}}_{1,j}z_{i}+{\bm{\beta}}_{2,j}(1-z_{i})\right)^{\alpha_{i,j}}\right)^{2}.

We next split a squared expectation into the expectation of the multiplication of two independent random variables:
\left(\mathbb{E}_{w}\left[w\right]\right)^{2}=\mathbb{E}_{w^{(1)}}\left[{w^{(1)}}\right]\mathbb{E}_{w^{(2)}}\left[{w^{(2)}}\right]=\mathbb{E}_{w^{(1)},w^{(2)}}\left[w^{(1)}w^{(2)}\right]
, where we have chosen
w^{(1)}
and
w^{(2)}
to be two independent and identically distributed random variables. Continuing in this way, we obtain:

\displaystyle\chi^{2}_{\leq D}({\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})\|{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}})\otimes{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{y}}))+1

\displaystyle=\sum_{d=0}^{D}\sum_{h=0}^{d}\sum_{|\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}|=h}\sum_{|\bm{\alpha}_{\cdot,:p}|=d-h}\frac{1}{\bm{\alpha}!}\left(\frac{\frac{1}{\sigma}}{\sqrt{\frac{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}}{\sigma^{2}}+1}}\right)^{2|\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}|}\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2|\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}|-2|\bm{\alpha}_{\cdot,:p}|}_{2}(\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}!)^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 0.0pt\cdot\underbrace{\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\alpha_{i,p+1}-|\bm{\alpha}_{i,:p}|=0\}}}_{\implies\sum_{i=1}^{n}\alpha_{i,p+1}-|\bm{\alpha_{i,:p}}|=0\implies 2h-d=0\implies d\text{ even}}

\displaystyle\cdot\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}({\bm{\beta}}_{1}^{(1)},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}^{(1)}),({\bm{\beta}}_{1}^{(2)},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}^{(2)})\mathbin{\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{P}\\
z^{(1)},z^{(2)}\mathbin{\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\text{Ber}(\phi)\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\prod_{j=1}^{p}\left({\bm{\beta}}^{(1)}_{1,j}z^{(1)}_{i}+{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)}_{2,j}(1-z^{(1)}_{i})\right)^{\alpha_{i,j}}\left({\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}_{1,j}z^{(2)}_{i}+{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}_{2,j}(1-z^{(2)}_{i})\right)^{\alpha_{i,j}},

which we simplify after noticing
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\alpha_{i,p+1}-|\bm{\alpha_{i,:p}}|=0
implies
d
must be even,

\displaystyle\chi^{2}_{\leq D}({\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})\|{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}})\otimes{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{y}}))+1

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{\frac{d}{2}=0}^{\lfloor\frac{D}{2}\rfloor}\sum_{|\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}|=\frac{d}{2}}\sum_{|\bm{\alpha}_{\cdot,:p}|=\frac{d}{2}}\frac{1}{\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}!\cdot\bm{\alpha}_{\cdot,:p}!}\left(\frac{1}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{0}_{2}(\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}!)^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\alpha_{i,p+1}-|\bm{\alpha}_{i,:p}|=0\}}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{n}\prod_{j=1}^{p}\left({\bm{\beta}}^{(1)}_{1,j}z^{(1)}_{i}+{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)}_{2,j}(1-z^{(1)}_{i})\right)^{\alpha_{i,j}}\left({\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}_{1,j}z^{(2)}_{i}+{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}_{2,j}(1-z^{(2)}_{i})\right)^{\alpha_{i,j}}

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{\frac{d}{2}=0}^{\lfloor\frac{D}{2}\rfloor}\left(\frac{1}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\cdot\sum_{|\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}|=\frac{d}{2}}\frac{(\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}!)^{2}}{\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}!}\sum_{|\bm{\alpha}_{\cdot,:p}|=\frac{d}{2}}\frac{1}{\bm{\alpha}_{\cdot,:p}!}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\alpha_{i,p+1}-|\bm{\alpha}_{i,:p}|=0\}}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{n}\prod_{j=1}^{p}\left({\bm{\beta}}^{(1)}_{1,j}z^{(1)}_{i}+{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)}_{2,j}(1-z^{(1)}_{i})\right)^{\alpha_{i,j}}\left({\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}_{1,j}z^{(2)}_{i}+{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}_{2,j}(1-z^{(2)}_{i})\right)^{\alpha_{i,j}},

and can be re-ordered in order to more clearly apply the multinomial theorem:

\displaystyle\chi^{2}_{\leq D}({\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})\|{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}})\otimes{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{y}}))+1

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{\frac{d}{2}=0}^{\lfloor\frac{D}{2}\rfloor}\left(\frac{1}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\sum_{|\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}|=\frac{d}{2}}\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}!

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\cdot\sum_{|\bm{\alpha}_{1,:p}|=\alpha_{1,p+1}}\frac{1}{\bm{\alpha}_{1,:p}!}\ldots\sum_{|\bm{\alpha}_{n,:p}|=\alpha_{n,p+1}}\frac{1}{\bm{\alpha}_{n,:p}!}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\prod_{i=1}^{n}\prod_{j=1}^{p}\left({\bm{\beta}}^{(1)}_{1,j}z^{(1)}_{i}+{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)}_{2,j}(1-z^{(1)}_{i})\right)^{\alpha_{i,j}}\left({\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}_{1,j}z^{(2)}_{i}+{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}_{2,j}(1-z^{(2)}_{i})\right)^{\alpha_{i,j}}

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{\frac{d}{2}=0}^{\lfloor\frac{D}{2}\rfloor}\left(\frac{1}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\sum_{|\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}|=\frac{d}{2}}\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}!

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\cdot\sum_{|\bm{\alpha}_{1,:p}|=\alpha_{1,p+1}}\frac{\prod_{j=1}^{p}\left({\bm{\beta}}^{(1)}_{1,j}z^{(1)}_{i}+{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)}_{2,j}(1-z^{(1)}_{i})\right)^{\alpha_{1,j}}\left({\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}_{1,j}z^{(2)}_{i}+{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}_{2,j}(1-z^{(2)}_{i})\right)^{\alpha_{1,j}}}{\bm{\alpha}_{1,:p}!}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\ldots\sum_{|\bm{\alpha}_{n,:p}|=\alpha_{n,p+1}}\frac{\prod_{j=1}^{p}\left({\bm{\beta}}^{(1)}_{1,j}z^{(1)}_{i}+{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)}_{2,j}(1-z^{(1)}_{i})\right)^{\alpha_{n,j}}\left({\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}_{1,j}z^{(2)}_{i}+{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}_{2,j}(1-z^{(2)}_{i})\right)^{\alpha_{n,j}}}{\bm{\alpha}_{n,:p}!}.

We then apply the multinomial theorem to obtain the result:

\displaystyle\chi^{2}_{\leq D}({\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})\|{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}})\otimes{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{y}}))+1

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{\frac{d}{2}=0}^{\lfloor\frac{D}{2}\rfloor}\left(\frac{1}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\sum_{|\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}|=\frac{d}{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\langle{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)}_{1}z^{(1)}_{i}+{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)}_{2}(1-z^{(1)}_{i}),{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}_{1}z^{(2)}_{i}+{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}_{2}(1-z^{(2)}_{i})\rangle^{\alpha_{i,p+1}}.

∎

In the next two subsections, we prove the computational lower bounds for
\mathtt{SLR-D}
(Theorem
2.8
and
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR-D}
(Theorem
2.2
) by specializing Lemma
B.9
.

B.2
Special Case:
\mathtt{SLR-D}

Proof of Theorem
2.8
.

In Theorem
B.10
below, recalling that
\texttt{SNR}=\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}/\sigma^{2}
, we let
n=(1-\epsilon)(1-2\theta)\frac{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|_{2}^{2}}{{\bm{\beta}}\|_{\infty}^{2}}\frac{\texttt{SNR}+1}{\texttt{SNR}}\log p
. Then Theorem
B.10
implies that for all
D\leq\frac{2\epsilon}{1-\epsilon}n
, we have

\chi^{2}_{\leq D}({\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})\|{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}})\otimes{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{y}}))=O(1)
. Applying Conjecture
1.2
with
D=\frac{2\epsilon}{1-\epsilon}n
and recalling
n=\omega(\log p)
(by the assumptions of the theorem), we have that running time
\exp\left(\tilde{\Omega}(n)\right)
is required.
∎

Theorem B.10

(General
\mathtt{SLR-D}
lower bound)
.

Consider the setting of
\mathtt{SLR-D}
(Definition
2.1
with
{\bm{\beta}}_{1}={\bm{\beta}}_{2}
). Let
{\bm{\beta}}\mathbin{\overset{}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{P}_{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|_{2}}(\mathcal{D})
. If
k=O(p^{\theta})\leq\sqrt{p}
for some
\theta\in(0,1/2]
, then for any
\epsilon\in(0,1)
,
n\leq(1-\epsilon)(1-2\theta)\left(\frac{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|_{\infty}^{2}}\right)\log{p}
and
D\leq\frac{2\epsilon}{1-\epsilon}n
, we have
\chi^{2}_{\leq D}({\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})\|{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}})\otimes{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{y}}))=O(1)
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{S}^{(1)}
and
\mathcal{S}^{(2)}
denote the support sets of
{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)}
and
{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}
, respectively. We apply Lemma
B.9
to obtain:

\displaystyle\chi^{2}_{\leq D}({\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})\|{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}})\otimes{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{y}}))+1

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{\frac{d}{2}=0}^{\lfloor\frac{D}{2}\rfloor}\left(\frac{1}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\sum_{|\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}|=\frac{d}{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\langle{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)},{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}\rangle^{\alpha_{i,p+1}}

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{\frac{d}{2}=0}^{\lfloor\frac{D}{2}\rfloor}\left(\frac{1}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\sum_{|\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}|=\frac{d}{2}}\langle{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)},{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}\rangle^{\frac{d}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{\frac{d}{2}=0}^{\lfloor\frac{D}{2}\rfloor}\left(\frac{1}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}{{\frac{D}{2}+n-1}\choose{n-1}}\langle{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)},{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}\rangle^{\frac{d}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{\frac{d}{2}=0}^{\lfloor\frac{D}{2}\rfloor}\left(\frac{1}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\frac{\left(\frac{D}{2}+n\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}}{\frac{d}{2}!}\langle{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)},{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}\rangle^{\frac{d}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)},{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}\mathbin{\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{P}}\exp\left(\frac{\frac{1}{\sigma^{2}}}{\frac{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}}{\sigma^{2}}+1}\left(\frac{D}{2}+n\right)\langle{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)},{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)},{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}\mathbin{\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{P}}\exp\left(\frac{\langle{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)},{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}\rangle}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|_{\infty}^{2}}(1-2\theta)\log{p}\right).

We then apply Lemma
B.7
and notice that
\langle{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)},{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}\rangle\leq\|{\bm{\beta}}\|_{\infty}^{2}|\mathcal{S}^{(1)}\cap\mathcal{S}^{(2)}|
to obtain, for
p>4
and
k\leq p
:

\displaystyle\chi^{2}_{\leq D}({\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})\|{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}})\otimes{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{y}}))+1

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)},{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}\mathbin{\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{P}}\exp\left(\frac{\langle{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)},{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}\rangle}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|_{\infty}^{2}}(1-2\theta)\log{p}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{\langle{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)},{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}\rangle}\exp\left(|\mathcal{S}^{(1)}\cap\mathcal{S}^{(2)}|(1-2\theta)\log{p}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=0}^{k}\frac{{k\choose l}{{p-k}\choose{k-l}}}{{p\choose k}}\exp\left(l(1-2\theta)\log{p}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=0}^{k}\frac{4k!}{p^{k}}\frac{k^{l}}{l!}\frac{(p-k)^{k-l}}{(k-l)!}\exp\left(l(1-2\theta)\log{p}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 4\sum_{l=0}^{k}\left(\frac{k^{2}}{p}\right)^{l}\exp\left(l(1-2\theta)\log{p}\right)

\displaystyle=4\sum_{l=0}^{k}\left(\frac{k^{2}}{p^{2\theta}}\right)^{l}=O(1).

∎

B.3
Special Case:
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR-D}

Proof of Theorem
2.2
.

This follows from Theorem
B.12
below. Choosing any sample size
n
such that
n\geq k
,
n=\omega(\log{p})
, and
n=o((\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})^{2}/(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|_{\infty}^{4}\log{p}))
, we have that
\chi^{2}_{\leq D}({\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})\|{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}})\otimes{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{y}}))=O(1)
for
D=(\sqrt{2}-1)\min\left\{\frac{(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})^{2}}{n\|{\bm{\beta}}\|_{\infty}^{4}},n\right\}
.
We then invoke Conjecture
1.2
and use
\frac{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}}{\sigma^{2}}=\texttt{SNR}
, and notice that for signals with bounded amplitude, we have
k\|{\bm{\beta}}\|_{\infty}/\sigma^{2}\gtrsim\texttt{SNR}\gtrsim k\|{\bm{\beta}}\|_{\infty}/\sigma^{2}
.
∎

Lemma B.11
.

For
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR-D}
, we have that:

\chi^{2}_{\leq D}({\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})\|{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}})\otimes{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{y}}))+1\leq\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)},{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}}\sum_{\frac{d}{4}=0}^{\lfloor\frac{D}{4}\rfloor}\left(\frac{1}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\frac{\left(\frac{D}{4}+n\right)^{\frac{d}{4}}}{\frac{d}{4}!}\langle{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)},{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}\rangle^{\frac{d}{2}},

where
{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)},{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}
are two independent copies of the random variable
{\bm{\beta}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle d}}{{=}}{\bm{\beta}}_{1}
.

Proof.

We begin by applying the assumptions into Lemma
B.9
and applying independence of the
z_{i}
’s. Notice that in the context of
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR}
, we have in particular that
\phi{\bm{\beta}}_{1}+(1-\phi){\bm{\beta}}_{2}=0
, and hence we plug in
{\bm{\beta}}:={\bm{\beta}}_{1}=-\frac{\phi}{1-\phi}{\bm{\beta}}_{2}
.

\displaystyle\chi^{2}_{\leq D}({\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})\|{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}})\otimes{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{y}}))+1

\displaystyle\leq\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\sum_{\frac{d}{2}=0}^{\lfloor\frac{D}{2}\rfloor}\left(\frac{1}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\sum_{|\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}|=\frac{d}{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\langle{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)}_{1}z^{(1)}_{i}+{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)}_{2}(1-z^{(1)}_{i}),{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}_{1}z^{(2)}_{i}+{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}_{2}(1-z^{(2)}_{i})\rangle^{\alpha_{i,p+1}}

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{\beta}}}\sum_{\frac{d}{2}=0}^{\lfloor\frac{D}{2}\rfloor}\left(\frac{1}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\sum_{|\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}|=\frac{d}{2}}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\phi^{2}\langle{\bm{\beta}}_{1}^{(1)},{\bm{\beta}}_{1}^{(2)}\rangle^{\alpha_{i,p+1}}+\,\phi(1-\phi)\langle{\bm{\beta}}_{1}^{(1)},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}^{(2)}\rangle^{\alpha_{i,p+1}}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 40.00006pt\left.+\ \phi(1-\phi)\langle{\bm{\beta}}_{2}^{(1)},{\bm{\beta}}_{1}^{(2)}\rangle^{\alpha_{i,p+1}}+(1-\phi)^{2}\langle{\bm{\beta}}_{2}^{(1)},{\bm{\beta}}_{1}^{(2)}\rangle^{\alpha_{i,p+1}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{\beta}}}\sum_{\frac{d}{2}=0}^{\lfloor\frac{D}{2}\rfloor}\left(\frac{1}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\sum_{|\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}|=\frac{d}{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 20.00003pt\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\phi^{2}+2\phi(1-\phi)\left(-\frac{\phi}{1-\phi}\right)^{\alpha_{i,p+1}}+(1-\phi)^{2}\left(\frac{\phi}{1-\phi}\right)^{2\alpha_{i,p+1}}\right)\langle{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)},{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}\rangle^{\alpha_{i,p+1}}

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{\beta}}}\sum_{\frac{d}{2}=0}^{\lfloor\frac{D}{2}\rfloor}\left(\frac{1}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\sum_{|\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}|=\frac{d}{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\left(\phi+(1-\phi)\left(-\frac{\phi}{1-\phi}\right)^{\alpha_{i,p+1}}\right)^{2}\langle{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)},{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}\rangle^{\alpha_{i,p+1}}.

We now notice that the term inside of the product equals zero for all
\alpha_{i,p+1}
odd if and only if
\phi=1/2
, which is the case for
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR}
. So we sum only over even terms to obtain:

\displaystyle\chi^{2}_{\leq D}({\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})\|{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}})\otimes{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{y}}))+1

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{\beta}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\frac{d}{2}=0\\
\text{even}\end{subarray}}^{\lfloor\frac{D}{2}\rfloor}\left(\frac{1}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}|=\frac{d}{2}\\
\text{even}\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\langle{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)},{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}\rangle^{\alpha_{i,p+1}}

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{\beta}}}\sum_{\frac{d}{4}=0}^{\lfloor\frac{D}{4}\rfloor}\left(\frac{1}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}|\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}|=\frac{d}{2}\\
\text{even}\end{subarray}}\langle{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)},{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}\rangle^{|\bm{\alpha}_{p+1}|}

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{\beta}}}\sum_{\frac{d}{4}=0}^{\lfloor\frac{D}{4}\rfloor}\left(\frac{1}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}{{\frac{d}{4}+n-1}\choose{n-1}}\langle{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)},{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}\rangle^{\frac{d}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{\beta}}}\sum_{\frac{d}{4}=0}^{\lfloor\frac{D}{4}\rfloor}\left(\frac{1}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}}\frac{\left(\frac{D}{4}+n\right)^{\frac{d}{4}}}{\frac{d}{4}!}\langle{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)},{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}\rangle^{\frac{d}{2}}.

∎

Theorem B.12

(General
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR-D}
lower bound)
.

Consider the setting of
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR-D}
with joint prior
\mathcal{P}_{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|_{2}}(\mathcal{D})
. If
k\leq\sqrt{\frac{p}{e}}
, and
k\leq D\leq 2(\sqrt{2}-1)\min\left\{\frac{(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})^{2}}{n\|{\bm{\beta}}\|_{\infty}^{4}},n\right\}
, we have that
\chi^{2}_{\leq D}({\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})\|{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}})\otimes{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{y}}))=O(1)
.

Proof.

We first apply the result of Lemma
B.9
to obtain:

\displaystyle\chi^{2}_{\leq D}({\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})\|{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}})\otimes{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{y}}))+1\leq\sum_{\frac{d}{4}=0}^{\frac{D}{4}}\left(\frac{1}{(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})^{2}}\right)^{\frac{d}{4}}\frac{\left(\frac{D}{4}+n\right)^{\frac{d}{4}}}{\frac{d}{4}!}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)},{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}\mathbin{\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{P}}\langle{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)},{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}\rangle^{\frac{d}{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 30.00005pt\leq\sum_{\frac{d}{4}=0}^{\frac{D}{4}}\left(\frac{1}{(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})^{2}}\right)^{\frac{d}{4}}\frac{\left(\frac{D}{4}+n\right)^{\frac{d}{4}}}{\frac{d}{4}!}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)},{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}\mathbin{\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{P}}\langle\texttt{abs}({\bm{\beta}}^{(1)}),\texttt{abs}({\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})\rangle^{\frac{d}{2}},

where
\langle{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)},{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}\rangle\leq\langle\texttt{abs}({\bm{\beta}}^{(1)}),\texttt{abs}({\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})\rangle
, and we recall
\texttt{abs}({\bm{\beta}})
denotes the entry-wise absolute value operation on the vector
{\bm{\beta}}
. Notice that by Lemma
B.7
we have, for
p>4,k\leq\sqrt{p}
,

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bm{\beta}}^{(1)},{\bm{\beta}}^{(2)}\mathbin{\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{P}}\langle\texttt{abs}({\bm{\beta}}^{(1)}),\texttt{abs}({\bm{\beta}}^{(2)})\rangle^{\frac{d}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=0}^{k}\frac{{k\choose l}{{p-k}\choose{k-l}}}{{p\choose k}}(l\|{\bm{\beta}}\|_{\infty}^{2})^{\frac{d}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 4\sum_{l=0}^{k}\left(\frac{k^{2}}{p}\right)^{l}(l\|{\bm{\beta}}\|_{\infty}^{2})^{\frac{d}{2}}.

We then obtain:

\displaystyle\chi^{2}_{\leq D}({\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})\|{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}})\otimes{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{y}}))+1\leq 4\sum_{\frac{d}{4}=0}^{\frac{D}{4}}\left(\frac{1}{(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})^{2}}\right)^{\frac{d}{4}}\frac{\left(\frac{D}{4}+n\right)^{\frac{d}{4}}}{\frac{d}{4}!}\sum_{l=0}^{k}\left(\frac{k^{2}}{p}\right)^{l}(l\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{\infty})^{\frac{d}{2}}.

With the aim of bounding the right hand side, we enforce condition
i)
:
(D/4+n)D\leq(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})^{2}/\|{\bm{\beta}}\|_{\infty}^{4}
. After switching sums, this yields

\displaystyle\chi^{2}_{\leq D}({\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})\|{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}})\otimes{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{y}}))+1

\displaystyle\leq 4\sum_{l=0}^{k}\left(\frac{k^{2}}{p}\right)^{l}\sum_{\frac{d}{4}=0}^{\frac{D}{4}}\frac{\left(\frac{(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})^{2}\|{\bm{\beta}}\|_{\infty}^{4}}{D(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})^{2}\|{\bm{\beta}}\|_{\infty}^{4}}\right)^{\frac{d}{4}}}{\frac{d}{4}!}l^{\frac{d}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 4\sum_{l=0}^{k}\left(\frac{k^{2}}{p}\right)^{l}\exp\left(\frac{l^{2}}{D}\right).

We now enforce condition
ii)
:
k\leq D
to obtain the result,

\displaystyle\chi^{2}_{\leq D}({\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})\|{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}})\otimes{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{y}}))+1

\displaystyle\leq 4\sum_{l=0}^{k}\left(\frac{k^{2}}{p}\right)^{l}\exp\left(l\right)

\displaystyle=4\sum_{l=0}^{k}\left(\frac{k^{2}e}{p}\right)^{l}=O(1).

Note that conditions
i)
and
ii)
are satisfied for any
n>0
and
k\leq D\leq 2(\sqrt{2}-1)\min\left\{\frac{(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})^{2}}{n\|{\bm{\beta}}\|_{\infty}^{4}},n\right\}
.
∎

Appendix C
Proofs of Polynomial-Time Reductions

Consider signed support recovery in the
\mathtt{MSLR}
problem, where we seek to recover the support of
{\bm{\beta}}_{1}
and
{\bm{\beta}}_{2}
, along with the along with the signs of their entries. Take
({\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2})\mathbin{\overset{}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{P}_{\|\beta\|_{2}}(\{-1,1\})
, and let
\mathcal{S}_{1}:={\rm supp}({\bm{\beta}}_{1})=\{j\in[p]:{\bm{\beta}}_{1,j}\neq 0\}
, and
\mathcal{S}_{2}
defined similarly for
{\bm{\beta}}_{2}
. We study the computational hardness of the problem as we vary two parameters of our joint signal distribution, the
overlap

\xi
and the
signed overlap

\tau
respectively:

\xi=\frac{|\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{S}_{2}|}{k},\qquad\tau=\frac{\langle{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}\rangle}{|\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{S}_{2}|},

that are of constant order, i.e., do not scale with respect to
n,p,k
. Previous work
(
Gamarnik and Zadik 2022
)
studies exact support recovery in sparse linear regression and the computational hardness that arises from the overlap distribution of two identical copies of the signal. We extend the analysis by considering exact
signed
support recovery by varying the parameter
\tau
, which measures the relative frequency of
+1
and
-1
entries with the same index. Note that for
\xi=1,\tau=1
we have the usual
\mathtt{SLR}
problem, for
\xi=1,\tau=-1
we have the
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR}
regime, and importantly for
\phi=1/2,\tau\in(-1,1),\xi>0
we have the
\mathtt{PSB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR}
regime. We denote
\mathtt{MSLR}_{\xi,\tau}
and
\mathtt{MSLR-D}_{\xi,\tau}
as the
\mathtt{MSLR}
and
\mathtt{MSLR-D}
problems with the joint signal prior
\mathcal{P}_{\|\beta\|_{2}}(\{-1,1\})
constrained to signals
({\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2})
with overlap and signed overlap
\xi
and
\tau
respectively.

Using these definitions, we first form in Lemma
C.1
a polynomial-time reduction from
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR-D}
to exact signed support recovery in
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR}
within the scaling regime of Theorem
2.2
. Notice that
\mathtt{MSLR-D}_{1,-1}
is equivalent to the
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR-D}
problem. Next, we prove a polynomial-time reduction from exact signed support recovery in
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR}
to exact signed support recovery in
\mathtt{MSLR}_{\xi,\tau}
for
\tau\in(-1,1),\xi>0
(
\mathtt{PSB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR}
) within the scaling regime of Theorem
2.2
, proving that if exact signed support recovery can be achieved in
\mathtt{PSB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR}
, then it can also be achieved in
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR}
.

Combining the two arguments above, we have that solving exact signed support recovery in
\mathtt{PSB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR}
implies solving strong detection in
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR-D}
, which would contradict the implication in Theorem
2.2
that
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR-D}
cannot be solved in polynomial time, resulting in Theorem
C.3
. For more background on the logic of average-case reductions, we refer to
(
Brennan and Bresler 2020b
)
.

The reduction from
\mathtt{SLR-D}
to
\mathtt{SLR}
is nearly identical to that in Lemma
C.1
with the midly less restrictive condition that
\texttt{SNR}\geq 1
, and hence the proof is omitted.

Throughout the proofs, we use the following measure of recovery error for mixtures of linear regressions
(
Chen et al. 2014
)
:

\rho((\hat{{\bm{\beta}}_{1}},\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2}),({\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2})):=\min\left\{\left\|\hat{\bm{{\bm{\beta}}}}_{1}-\bm{{\bm{\beta}}}_{1}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\hat{\bm{{\bm{\beta}}}}_{2}-\bm{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2}\right\|_{2},\,\left\|\hat{\bm{{\bm{\beta}}}}_{1}-\bm{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\hat{\bm{{\bm{\beta}}}}_{2}-\bm{{\bm{\beta}}}_{1}\right\|_{2}\right\}.

This error measure takes into account recovery of the two signals up to relabelling. For vectors
a
,
b
,
\hat{a}
,
\hat{b}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
, define

\displaystyle\|(\hat{a},\hat{b})-(a,b)\|_{\infty}:=\min\left\{\|\hat{a}-a\|_{\infty}+\|\hat{b}-b\|_{\infty},\|\hat{b}-a\|_{\infty}+\|\hat{a}-b\|_{\infty}\right\}.

Notice that for
\epsilon\in[0,1)
and signals
({\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2})\mathbin{\overset{}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{P}_{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|_{2}}(\{-1,1\})
we have that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left[\rho((\hat{{\bm{\beta}}_{1}},\,\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2}),({\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}))>\epsilon\right]\to 0\iff{\mathbb{P}}\left[\|(\hat{{\bm{\beta}}_{1}},\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2})-({\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2})\|_{\infty}>\epsilon\right]\to 0,

Main Statements

We begin by defining the parameter regimes of interest:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{1}=\left\{(p_{i},n_{i},k_{i},\sigma_{i})_{i=1}^{\infty}\subset\mathbb{N}^{4}:\,p_{i}=\omega_{i}(1),k_{i}=o(\sqrt{p_{i}}),n_{i}=\omega(\max\{k_{i},\log{p_{i}}\})\,,\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 40.00006pt\left.n_{i}=o\left((k_{i}+\sigma_{i}^{2})^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{\log{p_{i}}}\right)\right\}.

(7)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{2}=\left\{(p_{i},n_{i},k_{i},\sigma_{i})_{i=1}^{\infty}\subset\mathbb{N}^{4}:\,p_{i}=\omega_{i}(1),k_{i}=o(\sqrt{p_{i}}),n_{i}=\omega(\max\{k_{i},\log{p_{i}}\})\,,\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 40.00006pt\left.n_{i}\gtrsim\frac{k_{i}\log{p_{i}}}{\log(1+\frac{k_{i}}{\sigma_{i}^{2}})}\right\}.

(8)

Notice that
\mathcal{C}_{1},\mathcal{C}_{2}
are both contained within the parameter regime where
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR-D}
encounters a computational barrier, as per Theorem
2.2
. The following lemmas consist of two sub-reductions which together give the reduction argument from
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR-D}
to exact recovery in
\mathtt{PSB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR}
.

Lemma C.1
.

Let
({\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2})\mathbin{\overset{}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{P}_{\|\beta\|_{2}}(\{-1,1\}),\texttt{SNR}=\omega(1)
. Given a sequence of parameters
\{(p_{i},n_{i},k_{i},\sigma_{i})\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
in
\mathcal{C}_{2}
for
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR-D}
and
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR}
, if for any
\epsilon>0
there exists a randomized polynomial-time algorithm
\mathcal{A}
for
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR}
producing
(\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{1},\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2})
with
\mathbb{P}\left[\|(\hat{{\bm{\beta}}_{1}},\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2})-({\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2})\|_{\infty}<\epsilon\right]\underset{(i\to\infty)}{\to}1
, then there exists a randomized polynomial-time detection algorithm
\mathcal{A^{\prime}}
for
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR-D}
with vanishing Type I+II errors as
i\to\infty
.

The proof of Lemma
C.1
is given in Section
C.1
.

Lemma C.2
.

Fix signal priors to be
\mathcal{P}_{\|\beta\|_{2}}(\{-1,1\})
. For any sequence of parameters
\{(p^{\prime}_{i},n^{\prime}_{i},k^{\prime}_{i},\sigma^{\prime}_{i})\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
in
\mathcal{C}_{1}
for
\mathtt{PSB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR}
with solution
{\bm{\beta}}^{\prime}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}^{\prime}_{2}
and problem instances
({\bm{X}}^{\prime},{\bm{y}}^{\prime})
, there exists a sequence of parameters
\{(p_{i},n_{i},k_{i},\sigma_{i})\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
in
\mathcal{C}_{1}
for
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR}
with solution
{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}
and problem instances
({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})
such that, for any randomized polynomial time algorithm
\mathcal{A^{\prime}}
for
\mathtt{PSB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR}
outputting
(\hat{{\bm{\beta}}^{\prime}}_{1},\hat{{\bm{\beta}}^{\prime}}_{2})
with

{\mathbb{P}}\left[\|(\hat{{\bm{\beta}}^{\prime}}_{1},\hat{{\bm{\beta}}^{\prime}}_{2})-({\bm{\beta}}^{\prime}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}^{\prime}_{2})\|_{\infty}>0\right]\to 0,

we can construct a second randomized polynomial time algorithm
\mathcal{A}
for
\mathtt{PSB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR}
outputting
(\hat{{\bm{\beta}}_{1}},\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2})
such that

{\mathbb{P}}\left[\|(\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{1},\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2})-({\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2})\|_{\infty}>0\right]\to 0.

The proof of Lemma
C.2
is given in Section
C.2
.

Theorem C.3

(Reduction from
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR-D}
to exact recovery in
\mathtt{PSB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR}
)
.

Consider the setting of
\mathtt{PSB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR}
(
2
) with joint signal prior
\mathcal{P}_{\|\beta\|_{2}}(\{-1,1\})
. Any randomized polynomial-time algorithm
\mathcal{A}
solving
\mathtt{PSB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR}
within parameter regimes
\mathcal{C}_{1}\cap\mathcal{C}_{2}
and with
\texttt{SNR}=\omega(1)
would contradict Theorem
2.2
.

Proof.

Suppose there exists a randomized polynomial-time algorithm
\mathcal{A}
solving exact recovery in
\mathtt{PSB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR}
with signals in
\{-1,0,1\}^{p}
and parameter regime contained in
\mathcal{C}_{1}
defined in (
7
). Then by Lemma
C.2
we would have a randomized polynomial time algorithm
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
solving exact recovery in
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR}
within this regime. By Lemma
C.1
, we would then consequently have a polynomial-time algorithm solving
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR-D}
in the scaling regime
\mathcal{C}_{1}\cap\mathcal{C}_{2}
,
which is contained in the scaling regime of Theorem
2.2
and hence contradicts Theorem
2.2
.
∎

Remark C.4
.

Note that the lower bounds
\frac{k\log{p}}{\log(1+\texttt{SNR})}
in constraint
\mathcal{C}_{1}
in (
7
) used in Theorem
C.3
are not restrictive as this is the information-theoretic minimal sample complexity for support recovery in
\mathtt{SLR}

(
Reeves et al. 2019
;
Gamarnik and Zadik 2022
;
Wang et al. 2010
)
.

C.1
Reduction from
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR-D}
to
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR}

We utilize a variant of a theorem in
Gamarnik and Zadik 2017
to construct our reduction, Lemma
C.5
. Consider the following optimization problem for
\sigma>0
:

\displaystyle\psi:=\min\quad

\displaystyle n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lVert\sigma{\bm{w}}-{\bm{X}}{\bm{\beta}}_{1}\odot{\bm{z}}-{\bm{X}}{\bm{\beta}}_{2}\odot(1-{\bm{z}})\rVert_{2}

s.t.

\displaystyle{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}\in\{-1,0,1\}^{p},\ {\bm{z}}\in\{0,1\}^{n}

(9)

\displaystyle\|{\bm{\beta}}_{1}\|_{0}=\lVert{\bm{\beta}}_{2}\rVert_{0}=k,

where
{\bm{X}}\mathbin{\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
, independent from
w_{i}\mathbin{\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
.

Lemma C.5
.

Let
\psi
be as defined in (
9
). For
\delta>0
we have:

\mathbb{P}\left[\psi\geq e^{-(1+\delta)/2}\exp\left(-\frac{2k(\log{p}+1)}{n}\right)\sqrt{k+\sigma^{2}}\right]\geq 1-e^{-\frac{\delta}{2}n}.

The proof follows by nearly identical arguments as that of Theorem 3.1 in
(
Gamarnik and Zadik 2017
)
and is hence omitted.

Proof of Lemma
C.1
.

Throughout the proof, we drop the
i
subscript in the parameters
(p_{i},n_{i},k_{i},\sigma_{i})
for convenience. We refer to
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR}
and
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR-D}
as
\mathtt{MSLR}_{\xi,\tau}
and
\mathtt{MSLR-D}_{\xi,\tau}
respectively, with
\xi=1,\tau=-1
. We take
{\rm P}:={\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})
to represent the planted measure in the formulation of

\mathtt{SLR-D}
, and
{\rm Q}:={\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}})\otimes{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{y}})
to represent the null measure. We emphasize that, since
{\bm{\beta}}\mathbin{\overset{}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{P}_{\|\beta\|_{2}}(\{-1,1\})
,
\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}
and
k
are interchangeable. As prescribed in the statement of the lemma, suppose that
{\mathbb{P}}\left[\rho((\hat{{\bm{\beta}}_{1}},\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2}),({\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}))<\epsilon\right]\underset{(i\to\infty)}{\to}1
for any
\epsilon>0
.

Define the two following events under the planted hypothesis
{\rm P}
:

\tilde{\Omega}_{1}:=\left\{\{\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{1}={\bm{\beta}}_{1},\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2}={\bm{\beta}}_{2}\}\cup\{\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2}={\bm{\beta}}_{1},\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{1}={\bm{\beta}}_{2}\}\right\},

and

\tilde{\Omega}_{2}:=\{|w_{q}|<|\sigma^{-1}\langle{\bm{X}}_{q},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}-{\bm{\beta}}_{1}\rangle+w_{q}|,\>\>\forall q\in[n]\}.

Note that by assumption,
\tilde{\Omega}_{1}
occurs with probability
1-o(1)
under
{\rm P}
. Indeed, we can choose
\epsilon<1
in the definition of our given algorithm
\mathcal{A}
and since
\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{1},\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2},{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}\in\{-1,0,1\}^{p}
we obtain that
{\mathbb{P}}\left[\tilde{\Omega}_{1}\right]\to 1
.
We first consider the planted hypothesis
{\rm P}
. Let
\nu_{q}\mathbin{\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,(1-\xi\tau)\frac{k}{\sigma^{2}})
, and
g_{q}\mathbin{\overset{}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
, independent from each other and from
w_{q}
, for
q\in[n]
. In this case we have by symmetry that

\displaystyle{\rm P}\left[\tilde{\Omega}_{2}^{\complement}\middle|\tilde{\Omega}_{1}\right]

\displaystyle={\rm P}\left[\{|w_{q}|\geq|\sigma^{-1}\langle{\bm{X}}_{q},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}-{\bm{\beta}}_{1}\rangle+w_{q}|,\>\>\forall q\in[n]\}\right]

\displaystyle=\int{\rm P}\left[\{|w_{q}|\geq|\nu_{q}+w_{q}|,\>\>\forall q\in[n]\}\middle|w_{q}\right]{\rm P}[dw_{q}]

\displaystyle=2\int_{0}^{\infty}{\rm P}\left[\nu_{q}\in[-2w_{q},0],\>\>\forall q\in[n]\}\middle|w_{q}\right]{\rm P}[dw_{q}]

\displaystyle=2\int_{0}^{\infty}{\rm P}\left[\{g_{q}\in[-2w_{q}/((1-\xi\tau)k/\sigma^{2}),0],\>\>\forall q\in[n]\}\middle|w_{q}\right]{\rm P}[dw_{q}],

where we have

{\rm P}\left[\{g_{q}\in[-2w_{q}/((1-\xi\tau)k/\sigma^{2}),0],\>\>\forall q\in[n]\}\middle|w_{q}\right]\to 0\;\;\text{ as }{k/\sigma^{2}\to\infty},

and
{\rm P}\left[\{g\in[-2w_{q}/((1-\xi\tau)k/\sigma^{2}),0],\>\>\forall q\in[n]\}\middle|w_{q}\right]\leq 1
, so we can apply the Dominated Convergence Theorem to obtain that

\displaystyle{\rm P}\left[\tilde{\Omega}_{2}^{\complement}\middle|\tilde{\Omega}_{1}\right]

\displaystyle=2\int_{0}^{\infty}{\rm P}\left[\{g_{q}\in[-2w_{q}/((1-\xi\tau)k/\sigma^{2}),0],\>\>\forall q\in[n]\}\middle|w_{q}\right]{\rm P}[dw_{q}]\to 0\text{ as }k/\sigma^{2}\to\infty.

We therefore have that
{\rm P}\left[\tilde{\Omega}_{2}\middle|\tilde{\Omega}_{1}\right]=1-o(1)
, and hence
{\rm P}\left[\left(\tilde{\Omega}_{1},\tilde{\Omega}_{2}\right)\right]=1-o(1)
.

Next, note that under the planted hypothesis
{\rm P}
and in the joint event
(\tilde{\Omega}_{1},\tilde{\Omega}_{2})
we have for indices
q
such that
z_{q}=1
:

\displaystyle|y_{q}-\frac{1}{\sigma}\langle{\bm{X}}_{q},\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{1}\rangle|

\displaystyle=|y_{q}-\frac{1}{\sigma}\langle{\bm{X}}_{q},{\bm{\beta}}_{1}\rangle|

\displaystyle=|w_{q}|

\displaystyle<|\frac{1}{\sigma}\langle{\bm{X}}_{q},{\bm{\beta}}_{1}-{\bm{\beta}}_{2}\rangle+w_{q}|

\displaystyle=|y_{q}-\frac{1}{\sigma}\langle{\bm{X}}_{q},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}\rangle|

\displaystyle=|y_{q}-\frac{1}{\sigma}\langle{\bm{X}}_{q},\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2}\rangle|.

An analogous statement with
\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{1}
and
\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2}
swapped holds for indices
q
such that
z_{q}=0
. We therefore have that under
(\tilde{\Omega}_{1},\tilde{\Omega}_{2})
we can exactly estimate
{\bm{z}}
using the above thresholding procedure, and we call this exact estimate
\hat{{\bm{z}}}
.
We then define our detection algorithm in this case:

\mathcal{A^{\prime}}\left({\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{X}}\\
{\bm{y}}\end{bmatrix}}\right)=\begin{cases}\mathtt{p},&n^{-1/2}\|{\bm{y}}-\frac{1}{\sigma}{\bm{X}}\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{1}\odot\hat{{\bm{z}}}-\frac{1}{\sigma}{\bm{X}}\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2}\odot(1-\hat{{\bm{z}}})\|\leq\sqrt{5}\\
\mathtt{q},&n^{-1/2}\|{\bm{y}}-\frac{1}{\sigma}{\bm{X}}\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{1}\odot\hat{{\bm{z}}}-\frac{1}{\sigma}{\bm{X}}\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2}\odot(1-\hat{{\bm{z}}})\|>\sqrt{5}\end{cases}.

We will proceed to prove that
\mathcal{A^{\prime}}
has vanishing Type II error. Indeed, under
{\rm P}
and under the high-probability event
(\tilde{\Omega}_{1},\tilde{\Omega}_{2})
we have:

\displaystyle\|{\bm{y}}-\frac{1}{\sigma}{\bm{X}}\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{1}\odot\hat{{\bm{z}}}-\frac{1}{\sigma}{\bm{X}}\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2}\odot(1-\hat{{\bm{z}}})\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\|\frac{1}{\sigma}{\bm{X}}{\bm{\beta}}_{1}\odot{\bm{z}}+\frac{1}{\sigma}{\bm{X}}{\bm{\beta}}_{2}\odot(1-{\bm{z}})+{\bm{w}}-\frac{1}{\sigma}{\bm{X}}\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{1}\odot\hat{{\bm{z}}}-\frac{1}{\sigma}{\bm{X}}\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2}\odot(1-\hat{{\bm{z}}})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}+\|\frac{1}{\sigma}{\bm{X}}{\bm{\beta}}_{1}\odot{\bm{z}}+\frac{1}{\sigma}{\bm{X}}{\bm{\beta}}_{2}\odot(1-{\bm{z}})-\frac{1}{\sigma}{\bm{X}}\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{1}\odot\hat{{\bm{z}}}-\frac{1}{\sigma}{\bm{X}}\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2}\odot(1-\hat{{\bm{z}}})\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}.

We therefore have

\displaystyle{\rm P}\left[\mathcal{A^{\prime}}\left({\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{X}}\\
{\bm{y}}\end{bmatrix}}\right)=\mathtt{q}\right]

\displaystyle={\rm P}\left[\mathcal{A^{\prime}}\left({\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{X}}\\
{\bm{y}}\end{bmatrix}}\right)=\mathtt{q}\middle|(\tilde{\Omega}_{1},\tilde{\Omega}_{2})^{\complement}\right]\cdot{\rm P}\left[(\tilde{\Omega}_{1},\tilde{\Omega}_{2})^{\complement}\right]+{\rm P}\left[\left\{\mathcal{A^{\prime}}\left({\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{X}}\\
{\bm{y}}\end{bmatrix}}\right)=\mathtt{q}\right\}\cap(\tilde{\Omega}_{1},\tilde{\Omega}_{2})\right]

\displaystyle\leq{\rm P}\left[\mathcal{A^{\prime}}\left({\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{X}}\\
{\bm{y}}\end{bmatrix}}\right)=\mathtt{q}\middle|(\tilde{\Omega}_{1},\tilde{\Omega}_{2})^{\complement}\right]\cdot{\rm P}\left[(\tilde{\Omega}_{1},\tilde{\Omega}_{2})^{\complement}\right]+{\rm P}\left[\left\{\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}>\sqrt{5n}\right\}\cap(\tilde{\Omega}_{1},\tilde{\Omega}_{2})\right]

\displaystyle\leq 1\cdot o(1)+{\rm P}\left[\left\{\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}>\sqrt{5n}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 1\cdot o(1)+e^{-n}=o(1),

where the last inequality is obtained using a standard chi-square large deviation tail bounds (Example 2.11 of
Wainwright 2019
):

\displaystyle{\rm P}\left[\|{\bm{w}}\|_{2}<\sqrt{n+2\sqrt{nt}+2t}\right]\geq 1-e^{-t},

and taking
t=\sqrt{n}
.

We now turn to showing that
\mathcal{A^{\prime}}
has vanishing Type I error. Under the null hypothesis
{\rm Q}
we have by definition of
\psi
in Definition
9
that

\|{\bm{y}}-\frac{1}{\sigma}{\bm{X}}\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{1}\odot\hat{{\bm{z}}}-\frac{1}{\sigma}{\bm{X}}\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2}\odot(1-\hat{{\bm{z}}})\|_{2}\geq\psi,

where we recall from Lemma
C.5
that

{\rm Q}\left[\psi\geq e^{-3/2}\exp\left(-\frac{2k(\log{p}+1)}{n}\right)\sqrt{k+\sigma^{2}}\right]\geq 1-e^{-n},

hence it would suffice to show that

e^{-3/2}\exp\left(-\frac{2k(\log{p}+1)}{n}\right)\sqrt{k+\sigma^{2}}>\sqrt{5}.

In order to do so, choose
n^{*}=\frac{4k(\log{p}+1)}{\log{(1+\frac{k}{\sigma^{2}})}-\log{5}-3}=\Theta(\frac{4k\log{p}}{\log(1+\frac{k}{\sigma^{2}})})
and notice that if the inequality holds for
n^{*}
, it must hold for all
n\geq n^{*}
since the left hand side is increasing with
n
. We plug in
n^{*}
to obtain

e^{-3/2}\exp\left(-1/2\log(1+\frac{k}{\sigma^{2}})+\log{\sqrt{5}}+3/2\right)\sqrt{1+2\frac{k}{\sigma^{2}}}=\frac{\sqrt{1+2\frac{k}{\sigma^{2}}}}{\sqrt{1+\frac{k}{\sigma^{2}}}}\sqrt{5}>\sqrt{5},

and therefore we have that

\displaystyle{\rm Q}\left[\mathcal{A^{\prime}}\left({\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{X}}\\
{\bm{y}}\end{bmatrix}}\right)=\mathtt{q}\right]

\displaystyle={\rm Q}\left[\|{\bm{y}}-\frac{1}{\sigma}{\bm{X}}\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{1}\odot\hat{{\bm{z}}}-\frac{1}{\sigma}{\bm{X}}\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2}\odot(1-\hat{{\bm{z}}})\|_{2}>\sqrt{5}\right]

\displaystyle\geq{\rm Q}\left[\psi>\sqrt{5}\right]

\displaystyle\geq{\rm Q}\left[\psi\geq e^{-3/2}\exp\left(-\frac{2k(\log{p}+1)}{n}\right)\sqrt{k+\sigma^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\geq 1-e^{-n}.

Importantly, note that
n
satisfies the constraints of
\mathcal{C}_{2}
.

∎

C.2
Reduction from
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR}
to
\mathtt{PSB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR}

Proof of Lemma
C.2
.

First, recall that the
\mathtt{PSB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR}
regime implies,

\phi=1/2\text{ and }{\bm{\beta}}_{1,j}=-{\bm{\beta}}_{2,j}\text{ for }j\in J\subseteq{\rm supp}({\bm{\beta}}_{1})\cap{\rm supp}({\bm{\beta}}_{2})\text{ with }C_{1}k\leq|J|\leq C_{2}k,

for some constants
1\geq C_{1},C_{2}>0
. Without loss of generality, we can take
|J|=Ck
for some constant
0<C\leq 1
, and all constants that follow can be lower bounded or upper bounded accordingly. In light of this,
\mathtt{PSB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR}
corresponds to
\mathtt{MSLR}_{\xi,\tau}
for some
\tau\in(-1,1)
and some
\xi>0
, where we recall that
\xi,\tau
are of constant order, i.e., do not scale with respect to
n,p,k
.

For brevity, we denote
{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}:=\text{$\mathtt{PSB}$-$\mathtt{MSLR}$}
and
{\mathfrak{P}}:=\text{$\mathtt{SB}$-$\mathtt{MSLR}$}
. Let
c=1-\frac{\tau\cdot\xi+1}{2}\in(0,1)
denote the proportion of matching non-zero entries with opposite sign between
{\bm{\beta}}_{1}
and
{\bm{\beta}}_{2}
(intuitively, this corresponds to the “hard” portion of the signal), and note that it is fixed. Note that this follows since
\tau\cdot\xi=\frac{\langle{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}\rangle}{k}
for
\xi>0
.

Given a sequence of parameters
\{p^{\prime}_{i},n^{\prime}_{i},k^{\prime}_{i},\sigma^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{1}
for
{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}
, consider the sequence of parameters
\{(p_{i},n_{i},k_{i},\sigma_{i})\}_{i=1}^{\infty}=\{(cp^{\prime}_{i},n^{\prime}_{i},ck^{\prime}_{i},\sigma^{\prime}_{i})\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
for
{\mathfrak{P}}
. Notice that
\{(p_{i},n_{i},k_{i},\sigma_{i})\}_{i=1}^{\infty}\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{1}
since (dropping the subscript
i
notation for convenience):

•

k=ck^{\prime}=o(cC\sqrt{p^{\prime}})=o(c^{3/2}C\sqrt{p})=o(\sqrt{p})

•

n=n^{\prime}=o\left((k^{\prime}+(\sigma^{\prime})^{2})^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{\log{p^{\prime}}}\right)=o\left((k+\sigma^{2})^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{\log{p}}\right)
,

•

n=n^{\prime}=\omega(\max\{k^{\prime},\log{p^{\prime}}\})=\omega(\max\{k,\log{p}\})
,

and hence the parameter regimes of
{\mathfrak{P}}
are also contained in
\mathcal{C}_{1}
.
For
i\in\mathbb{N}
, let
J=({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})
denote an instance of
{\mathfrak{P}}
with parameters
(p_{i},n_{i},k_{i},\sigma_{i})
, where we recall:

\displaystyle\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{X}}\\
{\bm{y}}\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{X}}\\
\frac{1}{\sigma}{\bm{X}}{\bm{\beta}}_{1}\odot{\bm{z}}+\frac{1}{\sigma}{\bm{X}}{\bm{\beta}}_{2}\odot(1-{\bm{z}})+{\bm{w}}\end{bmatrix}.

We now want to show that, given a sequence of parameters
\{p^{\prime}_{i},n^{\prime}_{i},k^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{1}
for
{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}
and a randomized polynomial-time algorithm
\mathcal{A^{\prime}}
solving it, we can construct a randomized polynomial-time algorithm
\mathcal{A}
solving
{\mathfrak{P}}
along the above parameter sequence
\{p_{i},n_{i},k_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{1}
.
We will construct our desired algorithm
\mathcal{A}
by composing
\mathcal{A^{\prime}}
with a pre- and post-processing step. Indeed, we let
\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{B}\circ\mathcal{A^{\prime}}\circ\mathcal{D}
, where we define
\mathcal{B}
and
\mathcal{D}
below.

First, let
\mathtt{RS}
denote the random variable that reshuffles entries of a given size
p
vector or the columns of a given
p
-column matrix according to a uniform shuffling of the index set
[p]
. Let
\mathtt{IRS}
denote the random variable which inverts this reshuffling process on a vector or matrix, such that
\mathtt{IRS}\circ\mathtt{RS}
is the identity operation. Let
\mathbbm{1}
denote the all-ones vector of any size (to be inferred from context). Let
\bar{\mathbbm{1}}_{1}
and
\bar{\mathbbm{1}}_{2}
denote two independent copies of a
(1-c)k^{\prime}
sparse vector in
\{0,1,-1\}^{(1-c)p^{\prime}}
. We now define our pre- and post-processing procedures Algorithms
1
,
2
, which can be seen to run in randomized polynomial time with respect to
p
.

;

Data:

{\bm{V}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times(1-c)/c\>p}\text{ with columns}\mathbin{\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,{\bm{I}}_{n})

;

\tilde{{\bm{y}}}\leftarrow{\bm{y}}+\frac{1}{\sigma}{\bm{V}}\mathbbm{1}
;

\tilde{{\bm{X}}}\leftarrow\mathtt{RS}{\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{V}}&{\bm{X}}\end{bmatrix}}
;

return

(\tilde{{\bm{X}}},\tilde{{\bm{y}}})

Algorithm 1

\mathcal{D}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})
, where
({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})
is an instance of
{\mathfrak{P}}(p,n,k,\sigma)

{\begin{bmatrix}\bar{\mathbbm{1}}_{1}\\
{\bm{\beta}}_{1}\end{bmatrix}}\leftarrow\mathtt{IRS}(\tilde{{\bm{\beta}}}_{1})
;

{\begin{bmatrix}\bar{\mathbbm{1}}_{2}\\
{\bm{\beta}}_{2}\end{bmatrix}}\leftarrow\mathtt{IRS}(\tilde{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2})
;

return

({\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2})

Algorithm 2

\mathcal{B}(\tilde{{\bm{\beta}}}_{1},\tilde{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2})
, where
(\tilde{{\bm{\beta}}}_{1},\tilde{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2})
are both in
\{0,1,-1\}^{p^{\prime}}

Proposition C.6
.

For
i\in\mathbb{N}
,
({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})
an instance of
{\mathfrak{P}}(p_{i},n_{i},k_{i},\sigma_{i})
, and
({\bm{X}}^{\prime},{\bm{y}}^{\prime})
an instance of
{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}(p^{\prime}_{i},n^{\prime}_{i},k^{\prime}_{i})
, we have that
\mathcal{D}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})\overset{d}{=}({\bm{X}}^{\prime},{\bm{y}}^{\prime})
.

Proof.

Let
(\tilde{{\bm{X}}},\tilde{{\bm{y}}}):=\mathcal{D}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})
. First note that
\tilde{{\bm{X}}}\overset{d}{=}{\bm{X}}^{\prime}
since
{\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{V}}&{\bm{X}}\end{bmatrix}}
has dimensions
n\times(p+\frac{1-c}{c}p)=n^{\prime}\times(cp^{\prime}+(1-c)p^{\prime})=n^{\prime}\times p^{\prime}
.
Next, note that we can decompose
\tilde{{\bm{y}}}
as follows:

\displaystyle\tilde{{\bm{y}}}

\displaystyle={\bm{y}}+\frac{1}{\sigma}{\bm{V}}\mathbbm{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma}{\bm{X}}{\bm{\beta}}_{1}\odot{\bm{z}}+\frac{1}{\sigma}{\bm{X}}{\bm{\beta}}_{2}\odot(1-{\bm{z}})+\frac{1}{\sigma}{\bm{V}}\mathbbm{1}+w

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma}{\bm{X}}{\bm{\beta}}_{1}\odot{\bm{z}}+\frac{1}{\sigma}{\bm{X}}{\bm{\beta}}_{2}\odot(1-{\bm{z}})+\frac{1}{\sigma}{\bm{V}}\mathbbm{1}\odot{\bm{z}}+\frac{1}{\sigma}{\bm{V}}\mathbbm{1}\odot(1-{\bm{z}})+w

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sigma}(\mathtt{RS}{\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{V}}&{\bm{X}}\end{bmatrix}})\left(\mathtt{RS}{\begin{bmatrix}\bar{\mathbbm{1}}_{1}\\
{\bm{\beta}}_{1}\end{bmatrix}}\right)\odot{\bm{z}}+\frac{1}{\sigma}\left(\mathtt{RS}{\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{V}}&{\bm{X}}\end{bmatrix}}\right)\left(\mathtt{RS}{\begin{bmatrix}\bar{\mathbbm{1}}_{2}\\
{\bm{\beta}}_{2}\end{bmatrix}}\right)\odot(1-{\bm{z}})+w

and hence
\tilde{{\bm{y}}}
is the output of a
\mathtt{MSLR}
model with size
cp^{\prime}+(1-c)p^{\prime}=p^{\prime}
signals
\left(\mathtt{RS}{\begin{bmatrix}\bar{\mathbbm{1}}_{1}\\
{\bm{\beta}}_{1}\end{bmatrix}},\mathtt{RS}{\begin{bmatrix}\bar{\mathbbm{1}}_{2}\\
{\bm{\beta}}_{2}\end{bmatrix}}\right)
containing
ck^{\prime}
non-zero opposing sign entries from
({\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2})
and
(1-c)k^{\prime}
remaining non-zero entries from appending
\bar{\mathbbm{1}}
, for a total support of size
k^{\prime}
. Additionally, these signals are linearly transformed by a design matrix that is i.i.d Gaussian and
n^{\prime}\times p^{\prime}
as mentioned above. The first and second point together imply that
\tilde{{\bm{y}}}
is the output of a
\mathtt{MSLR}_{\xi,\tau}
model and
\mathcal{D}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})=(\tilde{{\bm{X}}},\tilde{{\bm{y}}})\overset{d}{=}({\bm{X}}^{\prime},{\bm{y}}^{\prime})
.
∎

Following Proposition
C.6
, all that is left to show is that
{\mathbb{P}}[\|\mathcal{B}\circ\mathcal{A^{\prime}}\circ\mathcal{D}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})-({\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2})\|_{\infty}>0]\to 0
. Indeed, the following steps hold due to Proposition
C.6
and the definition of
\mathcal{B}
:

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{A^{\prime}}({\bm{X}}^{\prime},{\bm{y}}^{\prime})-({\bm{\beta}}^{\prime}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}^{\prime}_{2})\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\overset{d}{=}

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{A^{\prime}}(\mathtt{RS}({\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{V}}&{\bm{X}}\end{bmatrix}}),{\bm{y}}+\frac{1}{\sigma}{\bm{V}}\mathbbm{1})-\left(\mathtt{RS}{\begin{bmatrix}\bar{\mathbbm{1}}_{1}\\
{\bm{\beta}}_{1}\end{bmatrix}},\mathtt{RS}{\begin{bmatrix}\bar{\mathbbm{1}}_{2}\\
{\bm{\beta}}_{2}\end{bmatrix}}\right)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{A^{\prime}}\circ\mathcal{D}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})-\left(\mathtt{RS}{\begin{bmatrix}\bar{\mathbbm{1}}_{1}\\
{\bm{\beta}}_{1}\end{bmatrix}},\mathtt{RS}{\begin{bmatrix}\bar{\mathbbm{1}}_{2}\\
{\bm{\beta}}_{2}\end{bmatrix}}\right)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{B}\circ\mathcal{A^{\prime}}\circ\mathcal{D}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})-\mathcal{B}\left(\mathtt{RS}{\begin{bmatrix}\bar{\mathbbm{1}}_{1}\\
{\bm{\beta}}_{1}\end{bmatrix}},\mathtt{RS}{\begin{bmatrix}\bar{\mathbbm{1}}_{2}\\
{\bm{\beta}}_{2}\end{bmatrix}}\right)\right\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{A}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})-({\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2})\right\|_{\infty},

and hence:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left[\|\mathcal{A^{\prime}}({\bm{X}}^{\prime},{\bm{y}}^{\prime})-({\bm{\beta}}^{\prime}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}^{\prime}_{2})\|_{\infty}>0\right]\to 0\implies{\mathbb{P}}\left[\left\|\mathcal{A}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})-({\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2})\right\|_{\infty}>0\right]\to 0,

where
\mathcal{A}
runs in randomized polynomial time with respect to the input size
p
since it is a composition of randomized polynomial time procedures with respect to
p
.
∎

C.3
Reduction from
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR-D}
to
\mathtt{SPR}

In this section, we present a polynomial-time reduction from strong detection in noiseless
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR-D}
to exact support recovery in
\mathtt{SPR}
, for signals with non-zero entries in
\{-1,0,1\}^{p}
.
More specifically, we consider the following
symmetric

\mathtt{SLR}
problem.

Definition C.7

(
\mathtt{S-SLR}
)
.

For
{\bm{X}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times p}
,
{\bm{w}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and
{\bm{z}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, consider the model:

{\bm{y}}=g\left({\bm{X}}{\bm{\beta}}\right)+{\bm{w}},

where
\odot
denotes element-wise product between vectors,
X_{i,j}\mathbin{\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
,
w_{i}\mathbin{\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
,
{\bm{\beta}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{p}
each
k
-sparse, and
g:{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\to{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
a separable entry-wise even function (
g_{i}(x)=g_{i}(-x)
for
i\in[n]
and
x\in{\mathbb{R}}
). Given
({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})
the objective is to estimate
{\bm{\beta}}
.

We note that setting
g_{i}(x)=|x|
and
g_{i}(x)=x^{2}
yields two standard formulations of the phase retrieval problem with sparse signals (
\mathtt{SPR}
)
(
Candès et al. 2015
;
Liu et al. 2021
)
. We seek to show hardness within the parameter scaling regime of Theorem
2.2
in the noiseless case (
\frac{\texttt{SNR}+1}{\texttt{SNR}}=1
) , and hence we prove a reduction within the constraint set
\mathcal{C}_{3}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}_{3}=\left\{(p_{i},n_{i},k_{i},\sigma_{i})_{i=1}^{\infty}\subset\mathbb{N}^{4}:\exists C\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\,\text{ s.t. }\,p_{i}=\omega_{i}(1),k_{i}=o(\sqrt{p_{i}}),,\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 40.00006pt\left.n_{i}=\omega(\max\{k_{i},\log{p_{i}}\})\,n_{i}=o\left(k_{i}^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{\log{p_{i}}}\right)\right\}.

(10)

We note that
\mathcal{C}_{3}
is just
\mathcal{C}_{1}
in (
7
), but with the stricter constraint
n_{i}=o\left(k_{i}^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{\log{p_{i}}}\right)
.
We begin with a lemma, which we use to initiate our reduction in Theorem
C.9
.

Lemma C.8
.

Fix signal priors to be
\mathcal{P}_{\|\beta\|_{2}}(\{-1,1\})
. For any sequence of parameters
\{(p^{\prime}_{i},n^{\prime}_{i},k^{\prime}_{i},\sigma^{\prime}_{i})\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
in
\mathcal{C}_{3}
for
\mathtt{S-SLR}
with solution
{\bm{\beta}}^{\prime}
and problem instances
({\bm{X}}^{\prime},{\bm{y}}^{\prime})
, there exists a sequence of parameters
\{(p_{i},n_{i},k_{i},\sigma_{i}=0)\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
in
\mathcal{C}_{3}
for
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR}
(noiseless) with solution
{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}
and problem instances
({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})
such that, for any randomized polynomial time algorithm
\mathcal{A^{\prime}}
for
\mathtt{S-SLR}
producing
\hat{{\bm{\beta}}^{\prime}}
with

{\mathbb{P}}\left[\|\hat{{\bm{\beta}}^{\prime}}-{\bm{\beta}}\|_{\infty}>0\right]\to 0,

we can construct a second randomized polynomial time algorithm
\mathcal{A}
for
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR}
outputting
(\hat{{\bm{\beta}}_{1}},\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2})
such that

{\mathbb{P}}\left[\|(\hat{{\bm{\beta}}_{1}},\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2})-({\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2})\|_{\infty}>0\right]\to 0.

Proof.

We drop the subscript
i
notation for convenience. For brevity, let
{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}:=\mathtt{S-SLR}
, and
{\mathfrak{P}}:=\text{$\mathtt{SB}$-$\mathtt{MSLR}$}
. Given a sequence of parameters
\{p^{\prime}_{i},n^{\prime}_{i},k^{\prime}_{i},\sigma^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
in
\mathcal{C}_{3}
for
{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}
, consider the sequence of parameters
\{(p_{i},n_{i},k_{i},\sigma_{i})\}_{i=1}^{\infty}=\{(p^{\prime}_{i},n^{\prime}_{i},k^{\prime}_{i},0)\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
for
{\mathfrak{P}}
. Notice that
\{(p_{i},n_{i},k_{i},\sigma_{i})\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
in
\mathcal{C}_{3}
.
Let
({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})
be a problem instance of
{\mathfrak{P}}
with parameters
\{(p_{i},n_{i},k_{i},\sigma_{i})\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
, and recall that for noiseless
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR}
the observation is of the form
({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}}={\bm{X}}{\bm{\beta}}\odot(2{\bm{z}}-1))
. We apply a preprocessing step to construct
\tilde{{\bm{y}}}:=g({\bm{y}})+{\bm{w}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
were
{\bm{w}}\mathbin{\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
, done in randomized polynomial time. Notice that
({\bm{X}},\tilde{{\bm{y}}})
is now an instance of
{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}
, by virtue of
g
being symmetric with respect to sign flips.
We then run algorithm
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
on
({\bm{X}},\tilde{{\bm{y}}})
to obtain
\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}^{\prime}
with
{\mathbb{P}}\left[\|\hat{{\bm{\beta}}^{\prime}}-{\bm{\beta}}\|_{\infty}>0\right]\to 0
as per the problem statement. Without loss of generality setting
(\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{1},\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2})=(\hat{{\bm{\beta}}},-\hat{{\bm{\beta}}})
, we have an algorithm
\mathcal{A}
which yields
{\mathbb{P}}\left[\|(\hat{{\bm{\beta}}_{1}},\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2})-({\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2})\|_{\infty}>0\right]\to 0
.
∎

Theorem C.9

(Reduction from
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR-D}
to exact recovery in
\mathtt{SPR}
)
.

Consider the setting of
\mathtt{SPR}
with joint signal prior
\mathcal{P}_{\|\beta\|_{2}}(\{-1,1\})
. Any randomized polynomial-time algorithm
\mathcal{A}
solving
\mathtt{SPR}
in parameter regime
\mathcal{C}_{2}\cap\mathcal{C}_{3}
and
\texttt{SNR}=\omega(1)
would contradict Theorem
2.2
.

Proof.

We first reduce
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR-D}
to
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR}
within the constraint set
\mathcal{C}_{2}
(
8
), using Lemma
C.1
. We then reduce noiseless
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR}
to
\mathtt{SPR}
using Lemma
C.8
within the constraint set
\mathcal{C}_{3}
(
10
) by choosing
g(x)=|x|
or
g(x)=x^{2}
depending on the precise definition of
\mathtt{SPR}
. Throughout, we have let
\texttt{SNR}=\omega(1)
to satisfy Lemma
C.1
. Suppose there exists a randomized polynomial-time algorithm
\mathcal{A}
solving exact recovery in
\mathtt{SPR}
with signals with non-zero entries in
\{-1,0,1\}^{p}
, i.e.
{\mathbb{P}}\left[\|\hat{{\bm{\beta}}^{\prime}}-{\bm{\beta}}\|_{\infty}>0\right]\to 0
. Then by the aforementioned chain of reductions we would have a randomized polynomial time algorithm
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
solving strong detection in
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR-D}
with signals with non-zero entries in
\{-1,0,1\}^{p}
in the scaling regime
\mathcal{C}_{2}\cap\mathcal{C}_{3}
, which is included in the parameter regime stated in Theorem
2.2
and would hence contradict Theorem
2.2
.
∎

For completeness, we also include a reduction from
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR-D}
to a detection variant of
\mathtt{SPR}
in Theorem
C.11
.

Definition C.10

(Detection Variant
\mathtt{SPR-D}
)
.

For
{\bm{X}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times p}
,
\sigma>0
, and
{\bm{w}}^{(1)},{\bm{w}}^{(2)}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, consider the following hypothesis testing problem:

\displaystyle{{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}})\otimes{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{y}})}:\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{X}}\\
{\bm{y}}\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{X}}\\
\sqrt{\frac{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}}{\sigma^{2}}}|{\bm{w}}_{1}|+{\bm{w}}_{2}\\
\end{bmatrix}

\displaystyle{{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})}:\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{X}}\\
{\bm{y}}\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{X}}\\
\frac{1}{\sigma}|{\bm{X}}{\bm{\beta}}|+{\bm{w}}\end{bmatrix}

where
({\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2})\sim\mathcal{P}_{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|_{2}}(\mathcal{D})
, and
X_{i,j}\mathbin{\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
,
w_{i}\mathbin{\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
,
z_{i}\mathbin{\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\text{Bernoulli}(\phi)
. The task is to construct a function
f
which strongly distinguishes
{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}})\otimes{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{y}})
from
{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})
.

Theorem C.11
.

Fix signal priors to be
\mathcal{P}_{\|\beta\|_{2}}(\{-1,1\})
. For any sequence of parameters
\{(p^{\prime}_{i},n^{\prime}_{i},k^{\prime}_{i},\sigma^{\prime}_{i})\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
in
\mathcal{C}_{3}
for
\mathtt{SPR-D}
with signal
{\bm{\beta}}^{\prime}
and problem instances
({\bm{X}}^{\prime},{\bm{y}}^{\prime})
, there exists a sequence of parameters
\{(p_{i},n_{i},k_{i},\sigma_{i}=0)\}_{i=1}^{\infty}\subseteq\mathcal{C}_{3}
for
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR-D}
(noiseless) with signals
{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}
and problem instances
({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})
such that, for any randomized polynomial time algorithm
\mathcal{A^{\prime}}
solving strong detection in
{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}
, we can construct a second randomized polynomial time algorithm
\mathcal{A}
for solving strong detection in
{\mathfrak{P}}
.

Proof.

We drop the subscript
i
notation for convenience. For brevity, let
{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}:=\mathtt{SPR-D}
, and
{\mathfrak{P}}:=\text{$\mathtt{SB}$-$\mathtt{MSLR-D}$}
. Given a sequence of parameters
\{p^{\prime}_{i},n^{\prime}_{i},k^{\prime}_{i},\sigma^{\prime}_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
in
\mathcal{C}_{3}
for
{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}
, consider the sequence of parameters
\{(p_{i},n_{i},k_{i},\sigma_{i})\}_{i=1}^{\infty}=\{(p^{\prime}_{i},n^{\prime}_{i},k^{\prime}_{i},0)\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
for
{\mathfrak{P}}
. Notice that
\{(p_{i},n_{i},k_{i},\sigma_{i})\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
is in
\mathcal{C}_{3}
.
Let
({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})
be a problem instance of
{\mathfrak{P}}
with parameters
\{(p_{i},n_{i},k_{i},\sigma_{i})\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
, and recall that in noiseless
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR-D}
the observation in the alternative hypothesis is of the form
({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}}={\bm{X}}{\bm{\beta}}\odot(2{\bm{z}}-1))
. We apply a preprocessing step to construct
\tilde{{\bm{y}}}:=|{\bm{y}}|+{\bm{w}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
were
{\bm{w}}\mathbin{\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
, done in randomized polynomial time. Notice that under both hypotheses,
({\bm{X}},\tilde{{\bm{y}}})
is an instance of
{\mathfrak{P}}^{\prime}
, by virtue of
g
being symmetric with respect to sign flips (even). We can then run algorithm
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
on
({\bm{X}},\tilde{{\bm{y}}})
to solve the hypothesis testing problem of
{\mathfrak{P}}
on
({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})
.
∎

Appendix D
Proofs for efficient algorithms

D.1
\mathtt{CORR}
for support recovery in
\mathtt{MSLR}

Theorem D.1

(
\mathtt{CORR}
achieves joint support recovery in
\mathtt{MSLR}
)
.

Consider the general setting of
\mathtt{MSLR}
,
({\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2})\mathbin{\overset{}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{P}_{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|_{2}}(\mathcal{D})
. Let
\epsilon\in(0,1)
be the one used in
\mathtt{CORR}
. Then provided

n\geq\frac{32(1+\epsilon)}{\min\{\phi^{2}{\bm{\beta}}^{2}_{\texttt{min}},(1-\phi)^{2}{\bm{\beta}}^{2}_{\texttt{min}},\langle{\bm{\beta}}\rangle^{2}_{\texttt{min}}\}}\frac{k(\texttt{SNR}+1)}{\texttt{SNR}}\log{2p},

we have that
\mathtt{CORR}
outputs the exact joint support of signals
{\bm{\beta}}_{1}
and
{\bm{\beta}}_{2}
with probability at least
1-c_{1}(\frac{k}{p}+ke^{-c_{2}n}+\frac{k}{n}+\frac{1}{p^{c_{2}}})
for constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
.

Proof.

Let
\delta>0
to be chosen later, and for two sets
A
and
B
denote the symmetric difference
A\Delta B:=(A\setminus B)\cup(B\setminus A)
. Let
\tau:=\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}
and define the error event

\mathcal{E}:=\cup_{j\in\mathcal{S}_{1}\cup\mathcal{S}_{2}}\left\{\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{j},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|<\tau\right\}\cup\left\{\max_{q\in(\mathcal{S}_{1}\cup\mathcal{S}_{2})^{\complement}}\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{q},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|\geq\tau\right\}.

From here we partition the set of indices
j\in\mathcal{S}_{1}\cup\mathcal{S}_{2}
into two sets,

J_{a}:=\{j\in[p]:j\in\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{S}_{2}\},\qquad J_{b}:=\{j\in[p]:j\in\mathcal{S}_{1}\Delta\mathcal{S}_{2}\},

with respect to which we perform a union bound:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{E}\right]\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\cup_{j\in\mathcal{S}_{1}\cup\mathcal{S}_{2}}\left\{\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{j},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|<\tau\right\}\right]+\mathbb{P}\left[\max_{q\in(\mathcal{S}_{1}\cup\mathcal{S}_{2})^{\complement}}\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{q},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|\geq\tau\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\cup_{j_{a}\in J_{a}}\left\{\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{j_{a}},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|<\tau\right\}\right]+\mathbb{P}\left[\cup_{j_{b}\in J_{b}}\left\{\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{j_{b}},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|<\tau\right\}\right]+\mathbb{P}\left[\max_{q\in(\mathcal{S}_{1}\cup\mathcal{S}_{2})^{\complement}}\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{q},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|\geq\tau\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2k\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{j_{a}},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|<\tau\right]+2k\mathbb{P}\left[\left\{\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{j_{b}},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|<\tau\right\}\right]+\mathbb{P}\left[\max_{q\in(\mathcal{S}_{1}\cup\mathcal{S}_{2})^{\complement}}\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{q},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|\geq\tau\right],

(11)

where
j_{a}\in J_{a}
,
j_{b}\in J_{b}
, and
q\in\left(\mathcal{S}_{1}\cup\mathcal{S}_{2}\right)^{\complement}
. Analyzing the last term, we note that for
q\notin\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{S}_{2}
we have
\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{q},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\mathbin{\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
. We therefore have,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\max_{q\in(\mathcal{S}_{1}\cup\mathcal{S}_{2})^{\complement}}\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{q},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|\geq\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i)}}{{\leq}}\mathbb{P}\left[\max_{q\in(\mathcal{S}_{1}\cup\mathcal{S}_{2})^{\complement}}\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{q},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|\geq\sqrt{2\log{2p}}+\frac{\epsilon}{2\sqrt{8}}\sqrt{\log{2p}}\right]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle ii)}}{{\leq}}(2p)^{-\frac{1}{16}\left(\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)^{2}},

where
i)
follows from
\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)}\geq\sqrt{2}+\frac{\epsilon}{2\sqrt{8}}
, and
ii)
from a tail bound on the maximum of standard Gaussians (see
\mathbb{P}\big[\Omega^{\complement}_{2}(t)\big]
in Lemma
D.9
).
Applying Lemmas
D.3
and
D.2
respectively (and choosing
\delta>0
small enough to satisfy these) to the first two terms in (
11
) we obtain that, for some constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{E}\right]

\displaystyle\leq c_{1}\left(\frac{k}{p}+ke^{-c_{2}n}+\frac{k}{n}+\frac{1}{p^{c_{2}}}\right).

∎

The principal concentration lemmas.

Lemma D.2

(General Bound for
j\in\mathcal{S}_{1}\Delta\mathcal{S}_{2}
)
.

Consider the setting of
\mathtt{MSLR}
. Let
j^{*}\in\mathcal{S}_{1}\Delta\mathcal{S}_{2}
. Then if
n\geq\frac{32(1+\epsilon)}{\min\{\phi^{2},(1-\phi)^{2}\}{\bm{\beta}}^{2}_{\texttt{min}}}(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})\log{2p}
for any
\epsilon\in(0,1)
we obtain that

\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{j^{*}},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|\leq\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}\right]\leq\frac{1}{p}+4e^{-\frac{\delta^{2}n}{8}}+\frac{39}{\delta^{2}n}.

Proof.

Let
g_{i}\mathbin{\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
(independent from
{\bm{y}},{\bm{z}},{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}
) for
i\in[n]
and
\delta>0
to be chosen later. Without loss of generality, we can assume
j^{*}\in\mathcal{S}_{1}\setminus\mathcal{S}_{2}
. We begin by considering fixed
{\bm{\beta}}_{1}
,
{\bm{\beta}}_{2}
,
{\bm{y}}
and
{\bm{z}}
vectors, and hence the initial randomness of interest lies in the design matrix
{\bm{X}}
. Define

•

\sigma^{(1)}_{i}:=\frac{y_{i}}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\sqrt{1-\frac{{\bm{\beta}}^{2}_{1,j^{*}}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}}
,

•

\sigma^{(2)}_{i}:=\frac{y_{i}}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}
,

•

\mu^{(1)}_{i}:=\frac{y^{2}_{i}}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\frac{{\bm{\beta}}_{1,j^{*}}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}
,

•

\mu^{(2)}_{i}:=0
,

and notice that for
\tau\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[X_{j^{*},i}\leq\tau|{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},{\bm{y}},{\bm{z}}\right]=\mathbb{P}\left[z_{i}\left(\frac{y_{i}\cdot{\bm{\beta}}_{1,j^{*}}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}+\sqrt{1-\frac{{\bm{\beta}}^{2}_{1,j^{*}}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}}g_{i}\right)+(1-z_{i})g_{i}\leq\tau\middle|{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},y_{i},z_{i}\right],

which follows from Lemma
D.4
. We then have that

\mathbb{P}\left[X_{j^{*},i}\frac{y_{i}}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\leq\tau\middle|{\bm{y}},{\bm{z}}\right]=\mathbb{P}\left[z_{i}(\mu^{(1)}_{i}+\sigma^{(1)}_{i}g_{i})+(1-z_{i})(\mu^{(2)}_{i}+\sigma^{(2)}_{i}g_{i})\leq\tau\middle|{\bm{y}},{\bm{z}}\right],

from which it follows that for
\tau>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{j^{*}},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|\leq\tau\middle|{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},{\bm{y}},{\bm{z}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}z_{i}(\mu^{(1)}_{i}+\sigma^{(1)}_{i}g_{i})+(1-z_{i})(\mu^{(2)}_{i}+\sigma^{(2)}_{i}g_{i})\right|\leq\tau\middle|{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},{\bm{y}},{\bm{z}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}(z_{i}\mu^{(1)}+(1-z_{i})\mu^{(2)})+(z_{i}\sigma^{(1)}+(1-z_{i})\sigma^{(2)})g_{i}\right|\leq\tau\middle|{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},{\bm{y}},{\bm{z}}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{i)}{=}\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}(z_{i}\mu^{(1)}+(1-z_{i})\mu^{(2)})+g_{1}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(z_{i}\sigma^{(1)}_{i}+(1-z_{i})\sigma^{(2)}_{i})^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right|\leq\tau\middle|{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},{\bm{y}},{\bm{z}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[\left\{\sum_{i=1}^{n}(z_{i}\mu^{(1)}+(1-z_{i})\mu^{(2)})+g_{1}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(z_{i}\sigma^{(1)}_{i}+(1-z_{i})\sigma^{(2)}_{i})^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\tau\right\}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\quad\cap\left\{-\tau\leq\sum_{i=1}^{n}(z_{i}\mu^{(1)}+(1-z_{i})\mu^{(2)})+g_{1}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(z_{i}\sigma^{(1)}_{i}+(1-z_{i})\sigma^{(2)}_{i})^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\}\middle|{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},{\bm{y}},{\bm{z}}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{ii)}{\leq}\mathbb{P}\left[g_{1}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(z_{i}\sigma^{(1)}_{i}+(1-z_{i})\sigma^{(2)}_{i})^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\tau-\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}(z_{i}\mu^{(1)}+(1-z_{i})\mu^{(2)})\right|\middle|{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},{\bm{y}},{\bm{z}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[g_{1}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(z_{i}\sigma^{(1)}_{i}+(1-z_{i})\sigma^{(2)}_{i})^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\tau-\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{y^{2}_{i}/\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\cdot z_{i}{\bm{\beta}}_{1,j^{*}}\right|\middle|{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},{\bm{y}},{\bm{z}}\right]

\displaystyle=:\mathbb{P}\left[A\middle|{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},{\bm{y}},{\bm{z}}\right],

(12)

where
i)
holds by the closure of Gaussian random variables under finite sum, and
ii)
holds by symmetry of the Gaussian
g_{1}
and since
\mathbb{P}\left[B_{1}\cap B_{2}\right]\leq\min_{i\in\{1,2\}}\mathbb{P}\left[B_{i}\right]
.

Using the high probability events
\Omega_{1}(\delta),\Omega_{4}(\delta)
defined in Lemma
D.9
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{j^{*}},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|\leq\tau\right]=\int_{{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}}\int_{y,z}\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\frac{\langle X_{j^{*},i},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|\leq\tau\middle|{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},{\bm{y}},{\bm{z}}\right]d\mathbb{P}[{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},{\bm{y}},z]

\displaystyle\leq\int_{{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}}\int_{\Omega_{1}(\delta)\cap\Omega_{4}(\delta)}\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\frac{\langle X_{j^{*},i},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|\leq\tau\middle|{\bm{y}},{\bm{z}}\right]d\mathbb{P}[{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},{\bm{y}},z]

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}}\mathbb{P}\left[\left(\Omega_{1}(\delta)\cap\Omega_{4}(\delta)\right)^{\complement}\middle|{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}\right]d\mathbb{P}[{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}]

\displaystyle=\int_{{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}}\int_{\Omega_{1}(\delta)\cap\Omega_{4}(\delta)}\mathbb{P}\left[A\middle|{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},{\bm{y}},{\bm{z}}\right]d\mathbb{P}[{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},{\bm{y}},z]+\mathbb{P}\left[\left(\Omega_{1}(\delta)\cap\Omega_{4}(\delta)\right)^{\complement}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\int_{{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}}\int_{\Omega_{1}(\delta)\cap\Omega_{4}(\delta)}\mathbb{P}\left[A\middle|{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},{\bm{y}},{\bm{z}}\right]d\mathbb{P}[{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},{\bm{y}},z]+\mathbb{P}\left[\Omega^{\complement}_{1}(\delta)\right]+\mathbb{P}\left[\Omega_{4}^{\complement}(\delta)\right].

(13)

Now setting
\tau=\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}
and
n\geq\frac{32(1+\epsilon)}{\phi^{2}{\bm{\beta}}^{2}_{\texttt{min}}}(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})\log{p}
we obtain that for
(y,z)\in\Omega_{1}(\delta)\cap\Omega_{4}(\delta)
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[A\middle|{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},{\bm{y}},{\bm{z}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[g_{1}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(z_{i}\sigma^{(1)}_{i}+(1-z_{i})\sigma^{(2)}_{i})^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\tau-\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{y^{2}_{i}/\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\cdot z_{i}{\bm{\beta}}_{1,j^{*}}\right|\middle|{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},{\bm{y}},{\bm{z}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[g_{1}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(z_{i}\sigma^{(1)}_{i}+(1-z_{i})\sigma^{(2)}_{i})^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 100.00015pt-\left|\frac{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\phi{\bm{\beta}}_{1,j^{*}}-\sqrt{\frac{2(3+\delta)\log{2p}}{(1-\delta)(1+\sigma^{2}/\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2})}}\right|\middle|{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},{\bm{y}},{\bm{z}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[g_{1}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(z_{i}\sigma^{(1)}_{i}+(1-z_{i})\sigma^{(2)}_{i})^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 100.00015pt-\left|\sqrt{\frac{n(1-\delta)}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}}\phi{\bm{\beta}}_{1,j^{*}}-\sqrt{\frac{2(3+\delta)\log{2p}}{(1-\delta)(1+\sigma^{2}/\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2})}}\right|\middle|{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},{\bm{y}},{\bm{z}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[g_{1}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(z_{i}\sigma^{(1)}_{i}+(1-z_{i})\sigma^{(2)}_{i})^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 100.00015pt-\left|\sqrt{32(1+\epsilon)(1-\delta)\log{2p}}-\sqrt{\frac{2(3+\delta)\log{2p}}{(1-\delta)(1+\sigma^{2}/\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2})}}\right|\middle|{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},{\bm{y}},{\bm{z}}\right],

(14)

where for small enough
\delta>0
, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)}-\left|\sqrt{32(1+\epsilon)(1-\delta)}-\sqrt{2(3+\delta)/(1-\delta)(1+\sigma^{2}/\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2})}\right|\leq-\sqrt{2}.

(15)

Hence we obtain that the right hand side of the inequality in (
14
) is negative, leading us to the following inequality for
(y,z)\in\Omega_{1}(\delta)\cap\Omega_{4}(\delta)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[A\middle|{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},{\bm{y}},{\bm{z}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[g_{1}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(z_{i}\sigma^{(1)}_{i}+(1-z_{i})\sigma^{(2)}_{i})^{2}\right)^{1/2}\leq-\sqrt{2\log{2p}}\middle|{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},{\bm{y}},{\bm{z}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\frac{2\log{2p}}{2\sum_{i=1}^{n}(z_{i}\sigma^{(1)}_{i}+(1-z_{i})\sigma^{(2)}_{i})^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{i)}{\leq}\exp\left(-\frac{2\log{2p}}{2\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{y_{i}^{2}}{\|{\bm{y}}\|^{2}_{2}}}\right)\leq\frac{1}{2p},

(16)

where
i)
follows almost surely from the fact that
z_{i}\in\{0,1\}
and
\left(1-\frac{{\bm{\beta}}^{2}_{1,j^{*}}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}_{1}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right)\leq 1
. We generalize to the case where
j\in\mathcal{S}_{2}\setminus\mathcal{S}_{1}
, and hence consider the analogous result with
\phi
and
{\bm{\beta}}_{1}
replaced with
(1-\phi)
and
{\bm{\beta}}_{2}
. Putting it all together in (
13
) using Lemma
D.9
and choosing
\delta>0
small enough to satisfy (
15
), we obtain that for
n\geq\frac{32}{\min\{\phi^{2},(1-\phi)^{2}\}{\bm{\beta}}^{2}_{\texttt{min}}}(1+\epsilon)(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})\log{2p}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{j^{*}},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|\leq\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{p}+4e^{-\frac{\delta^{2}n}{8}}+\frac{39}{\delta^{2}n}.

(17)

∎

Lemma D.3

(General Bound for
j\in\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{S}_{2}
)
.

Consider the setting of
\mathtt{MSLR}
. Let
j^{*}\in\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{S}_{2}
. Then if
n\geq\frac{32(1+\epsilon)}{(\phi\beta_{1,j^{*}}+(1-\phi)\beta_{2,j^{*}})^{2}}(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})\log{2p}
for any
\epsilon\in(0,1)
we obtain that

\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{j^{*}},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|\leq\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}\right]\leq\frac{1}{p}+4e^{-\frac{\delta^{2}n}{8}}+\frac{39}{\delta^{2}n}.

Proof.

The proof is along the same lines as that of Lemma
D.2
, with the main difference being in the conditional means and variances of
X_{j^{*},i}
