, for
i\in[n]
. As before, let
g_{i}\mathbin{\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
(independent from
y,z,{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}
) for
i\in[n]
and
\delta>0
to be chosen later. Define

•

\sigma^{(1)}_{i}:=\frac{y_{i}}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\sqrt{1-\frac{{\bm{\beta}}^{2}_{1,j^{*}}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}}
,

•

\sigma^{(2)}_{i}:=\frac{y_{i}}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\sqrt{1-\frac{{\bm{\beta}}^{2}_{2,j^{*}}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}}
,

•

\mu^{(1)}_{i}:=\frac{y^{2}_{i}}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\frac{{\bm{\beta}}_{1,j^{*}}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}
,

•

\mu^{(2)}_{i}:=\frac{y^{2}_{i}}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\frac{{\bm{\beta}}_{2,j^{*}}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}
,

and notice that for
\tau\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[X_{j^{*},i}\leq\tau|{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},{\bm{y}},{\bm{z}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[z_{i}\left(\frac{y_{i}\cdot{\bm{\beta}}_{1,j^{*}}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}+\sqrt{1-\frac{{\bm{\beta}}^{2}_{1,j^{*}}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}}g_{i}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\left.+(1-z_{i})\left(\frac{y_{i}\cdot{\bm{\beta}}_{2,j^{*}}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}+\sqrt{1-\frac{{\bm{\beta}}^{2}_{2,j^{*}}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}}g_{i}\right)\leq\tau\middle|{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},y_{i},z_{i}\right],

which follows from Lemma
D.4
. We then have that

\mathbb{P}\left[X_{j^{*},i}\frac{y_{i}}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\leq\tau\middle|{\bm{y}},{\bm{z}}\right]=\mathbb{P}\left[z_{i}(\mu^{(1)}_{i}+\sigma^{(1)}_{i}g_{i})+(1-z_{i})(\mu^{(2)}_{i}+\sigma^{(2)}_{i}g_{i})\leq\tau\middle|{\bm{y}},{\bm{z}}\right].

Then, using the same steps as in (
12
), we obtain that for for
\tau>0
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{j^{*}},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|\leq\tau\middle|{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},{\bm{y}},{\bm{z}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[g_{1}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(z_{i}\sigma^{(1)}_{i}+(1-z_{i})\sigma^{(2)}_{i})^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\tau-\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{y^{2}_{i}/\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\cdot(z_{i}{\bm{\beta}}_{1,j^{*}}+(1-z_{i}){\bm{\beta}}_{2,j^{*}})\right|\middle|{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},{\bm{y}},{\bm{z}}\right]

\displaystyle=:\mathbb{P}\left[A\middle|{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},{\bm{y}},{\bm{z}}\right].

(18)

Using the high probability events
\Omega_{1}(\delta),\Omega_{3}(\delta)
defined in Lemma
D.9
, by the same arguments as in (
13
) we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{j^{*}},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|\leq\tau\right]

\displaystyle\leq\int_{{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}}\int_{\Omega_{1}(\delta)\cap\Omega_{3}(\delta)}\mathbb{P}\left[A\middle|{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},{\bm{y}},{\bm{z}}\right]d\mathbb{P}[{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},{\bm{y}},z]+\mathbb{P}\left[\Omega^{\complement}_{1}(\delta)\right]+\mathbb{P}\left[\Omega_{3}^{\complement}(\delta)\right],

(19)

Now setting
\tau=\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}
and
n\geq\frac{32}{\langle{\bm{\beta}}\rangle^{2}_{\texttt{min}}}(1+\epsilon)(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})\log{2p}
we obtain that for
(y,z)\in\Omega_{1}(\delta)\cap\Omega_{3}(\delta)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[A\middle|{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},{\bm{y}},{\bm{z}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[g_{1}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(z_{i}\sigma^{(1)}_{i}+(1-z_{i})\sigma^{(2)}_{i})^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\tau-\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{y^{2}_{i}/\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\cdot(z_{i}{\bm{\beta}}_{1,j^{*}}+(1-z_{i}){\bm{\beta}}_{2,j^{*}})\right|\middle|{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},{\bm{y}},{\bm{z}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[g_{1}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(z_{i}\sigma^{(1)}_{i}+(1-z_{i})\sigma^{(2)}_{i})^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 100.00015pt-\left|\frac{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}(\phi{\bm{\beta}}_{1,j^{*}}+(1-\phi){\bm{\beta}}_{2,j^{*}})-\sqrt{\frac{2(3+\delta)\log{2p}}{(1-\delta)(1+\sigma^{2}/\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2})}}\right|\middle|{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},{\bm{y}},{\bm{z}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[g_{1}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(z_{i}\sigma^{(1)}_{i}+(1-z_{i})\sigma^{(2)}_{i})^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 100.00015pt-\left|\sqrt{\frac{n(1-\delta)}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}}(\phi{\bm{\beta}}_{1,j^{*}}+(1-\phi){\bm{\beta}}_{2,j^{*}})-\sqrt{\frac{2(3+\delta)\log{2p}}{(1-\delta)(1+\sigma^{2}/\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2})}}\right|\middle|{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},{\bm{y}},{\bm{z}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[g_{1}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}(z_{i}\sigma^{(1)}_{i}+(1-z_{i})\sigma^{(2)}_{i})^{2}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 100.00015pt-\left|\sqrt{32(1+\epsilon)(1-\delta)\log{2p}}-\sqrt{\frac{2(3+\delta)\log{2p}}{(1-\delta)(1+\sigma^{2}/\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2})}}\right|\middle|{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},{\bm{y}},{\bm{z}}\right],

(20)

where

\displaystyle\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)}-\left|\sqrt{32(1+\epsilon)(1-\delta)}-\sqrt{2(3+\delta)/(1-\delta)(1+\sigma^{2}/\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2})}\right|\leq-\sqrt{2},

(21)

for small enough
\delta>0
. Hence we obtain that the right hand side of (
20
) is negative, leading us to the following inequality for
(y,z)\in\Omega_{1}(\delta)\cap\Omega_{3}(\delta)
, obtained via the same steps as (
16
):

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[A\middle|{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},{\bm{y}},{\bm{z}}\right]\leq\frac{1}{2p}.

Putting it all together in (
19
) using Lemma
D.9
and choosing
\delta>0
small enough to satisfy (
20
) we obtain that for
n\geq\frac{32}{\langle{\bm{\beta}}\rangle^{2}_{\texttt{min}}}(1+\epsilon)(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})\log{2p}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{j^{*}},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|\leq\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{p}+4e^{-\frac{\delta^{2}n}{8}}+\frac{39}{\delta^{2}n}.

∎

The conditioning lemmas.

Lemma D.4

(General conditioning lemma)
.

Consider the setting of
\mathtt{MSLR}
.

For
j^{*}\in\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{S}_{2}
and
i\in[n]
it holds that:

\displaystyle\left.X_{j^{*},i}\middle|(y_{i},{\bm{\beta}}_{1},z_{i}=1)\right.

\displaystyle\mathbin{\overset{}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{y_{i}\cdot{\bm{\beta}}_{1,j^{*}}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}_{1}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}},\left(1-\frac{{\bm{\beta}}^{2}_{1,j^{*}}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}_{1}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right)\right),

(22)

\displaystyle\left.X_{j^{*},i}\middle|(y_{i},{\bm{\beta}}_{2},z_{i}=0)\right.

\displaystyle\mathbin{\overset{}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{y_{i}\cdot{\bm{\beta}}_{2,j^{*}}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}_{1}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}},\left(1-\frac{{\bm{\beta}}^{2}_{2,j^{*}}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}_{1}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right)\right).

(23)

For
j^{*}\in\mathcal{S}_{1}\Delta\mathcal{S}_{2}
(without loss of generality
j^{*}\in\mathcal{S}_{1}\setminus\mathcal{S}_{2}
) and
i\in[n]
it holds that

\displaystyle\left.X_{j^{*},i}\middle|(y_{i},{\bm{\beta}}_{1},z_{i}=1)\right.

\displaystyle\mathbin{\overset{}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{y_{i}\cdot{\bm{\beta}}_{1,j^{*}}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}_{1}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}},\left(1-\frac{{\bm{\beta}}^{2}_{1,j^{*}}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}_{1}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right)\right),

(24)

\displaystyle\left.X_{j^{*},i}\middle|(y_{i},z_{i}=0)\right.

\displaystyle\mathbin{\overset{}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}\left(0,1\right)

(25)

Proof.

To prove (
22
) and (
24
), recall that

y_{i}=z_{i}({\bm{X}}{\bm{\beta}}_{1})_{i}+(1-z_{i})({\bm{X}}{\bm{\beta}}_{2})_{i}+w_{i},

and hence given
z_{i}=1
we have

y_{i}=\sum_{j\neq j^{*}\in\mathcal{S}_{1}}{\bm{\beta}}_{1,j}X_{j,i}+{\bm{\beta}}_{1,j^{*}}X_{j^{*},i}+w_{i},

implying that

\displaystyle\left.X_{j^{*},i}\,\middle|\,(y_{i},{\bm{\beta}}_{1},z_{i}=1)\right.

\displaystyle=\left.X_{j^{*},i}\,\middle|\,\left({\bm{\beta}}_{1},y_{i}=\sum_{j\neq j^{*}\in\mathcal{S}_{1}}{\bm{\beta}}_{1,j}X_{j,i}+{\bm{\beta}}_{1,j^{*}}X_{j^{*},i}+w_{i}\right)\right..

Therefore, applying Corollary
D.7
, we obtain that
\left.X_{j^{*},i}\middle|(y_{i},{\bm{\beta}}_{1},z_{i}=1)\right.\mathbin{\overset{}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{y_{i}{\bm{\beta}}_{1,j^{*}}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}_{1}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}},\left(1-\frac{{\bm{\beta}}^{2}_{1,j^{*}}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}_{1}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right)\right)
. The result in (
23
) is proved in the same way, but replacing
{\bm{\beta}}_{1}
with
{\bm{\beta}}_{2}
.

For (
25
), notice that given
z_{i}=0
we have

y_{i}=\sum_{j\in\mathcal{S}_{2}}{\bm{\beta}}_{2,j}X_{j,i}+w_{i},

which is independent of
X_{j^{*},i}
by definition, and hence
X_{j^{*},i}|(y_{i},z_{i}=0)
has the same distribution as
X_{j^{*},i}\mathbin{\overset{}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
.
∎

Lemma D.5

(General conditioning lemma)
.

Let
a\mathbin{\overset{}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}\left(0,\Sigma_{k\times k}\right)
, and
b\in\mathbb{R}^{k}
a fixed vector. Then:

\left.a\middle|\right.\left(\sum_{j=1}^{k}b_{j}a_{j}=\eta\right)\mathbin{\overset{}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}\left(\eta v,B\Sigma_{k\times k}B^{T}\right)

where, letting
\mathbbm{1}
denote the all-ones vector,

v=\frac{1}{b^{T}\tilde{\Sigma}\mathbbm{1}}\tilde{\Sigma}\mathbbm{1},\quad B=I_{k\times k}-vb^{T},\quad\tilde{\Sigma}=\mathbb{E}\left[a(a\odot b)^{T}\right].

Proof.

Let
B
be a deterministic matrix, and
\eta:=\sum_{j=1}^{k}b_{j}a_{j}
. Then
(Ba,\eta)
is jointly normal. We will construct a fixed matrix
B
and fixed vector
v
such that

•

Ba
is independent from
\eta

•

a=Ba+\eta v
.

If the above holds, then by independence we have the required result that
a|\eta\mathbin{\overset{}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}\left(\eta v,B\Sigma B^{T}\right)
.
In order for the first point to hold, their covariances must be zero, implying

\mathbb{E}\left[Ba\eta\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[Ba(a\odot b)^{T}\mathbbm{1}\right]=B\tilde{\Sigma}\mathbbm{1}=0.

Meanwhile, the second point is satisfied by choosing
B=I-vb^{T}
. Combining these two facts, we obtain the result.
∎

Corollary D.6
.

Consider the setting of
\mathtt{SLR}
, let
j^{*}\in\mathcal{S}
, and
i\in[n]
. Then it holds that

\left.X_{j^{*},i}\middle|\left(\sum_{j\in\mathcal{S}}X_{j,i}+w_{i}=\eta\right)\right.\mathbin{\overset{}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{\eta}{k+\sigma^{2}},\left(1-\frac{1}{k+\sigma^{2}}\right)\right)

Proof.

Apply Lemma
D.5
conditioning on the sum
\sum_{j\in\mathcal{S}}X_{j,i}+w_{i}=:\eta
, where we recall
\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{X}}_{\mathcal{S}}\;w_{i}\end{bmatrix}\mathbin{\overset{}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}\left(0,\Sigma\right)
with

\Sigma=\begin{bmatrix}1&0&\cdots&0\\
0&1&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
0&0&\cdots&\sigma^{2}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{(k+1)\times(k+1)}

yielding

v=\frac{1}{k+\sigma^{2}}\begin{bmatrix}1\\
1\\
\vdots\\
\sigma^{2}\end{bmatrix}

and

B=\begin{bmatrix}(1-\frac{1}{k+\sigma^{2}})&-\frac{1}{k+\sigma^{2}}&\cdots&-\frac{1}{k+\sigma^{2}}\\
-\frac{1}{k+\sigma^{2}}&(1-\frac{1}{k+\sigma^{2}})&\cdots&-\frac{1}{k+\sigma^{2}}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
-\frac{1}{k+\sigma^{2}}&-\frac{1}{k+\sigma^{2}}&\cdots&(1-\frac{1}{k+\sigma^{2}})\\
\end{bmatrix}.

Noticing that

(B\Sigma B^{T})_{j^{*},j^{*}}=1-\frac{1}{k+\sigma^{2}}
,
we obtain the result.
∎

Corollary D.7
.

Consider the setting of
\mathtt{SLR}
, and let
b\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
be fixed. Let
j^{*}\in\mathcal{S}
, and
i\in[n]
. Then it holds that

\left.X_{j^{*},i}\middle|\left(\sum_{j\in\mathcal{S}}b_{j}X_{j,i}+w_{i}=\eta\right)\right.\mathbin{\overset{}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}\left(\frac{\eta\cdot b_{j^{*}}}{\|b\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}},\left(1-\frac{b^{2}_{j^{*}}}{\|b\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right)\right)

Proof.

The result is obtained by applying Lemma
D.5
conditioning on the sum
\sum_{j\in\mathcal{S}}b_{j}X_{j,i}+w_{i}=\eta
. We then have that

\tilde{\Sigma}=\mathbb{E}\left[{\begin{bmatrix}X_{1,i}\\
X_{2,i}\\
\vdots\\
w_{i}\end{bmatrix}}{\begin{bmatrix}b_{1}X_{1,i}\\
b_{2}X_{2,i}\\
\vdots\\
w_{i}\end{bmatrix}}^{T}\right]=\begin{bmatrix}b_{1}&0&\cdots&0\\
0&b_{2}&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
0&0&\cdots&\sigma^{2}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{(k+1)\times(k+1)}

yielding

v=\frac{1}{\|b\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\begin{bmatrix}b_{1}\\
b_{2}\\
\vdots\\
\sigma^{2}\end{bmatrix}

and

B=\begin{bmatrix}(1-\frac{b^{2}_{1}}{\|b\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}})&-\frac{b_{1}b_{2}}{\|b\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}&\cdots&-\frac{b_{1}}{\|b\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\\
-\frac{b_{2}b_{1}}{\|b\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}&(1-\frac{b^{2}_{2}}{\|b\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}})&\cdots&-\frac{b_{2}}{\|b\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
-\frac{\sigma^{2}b_{1}}{\|b\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}&-\frac{\sigma^{2}b_{2}}{\|b\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}&\cdots&(1-\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\|b\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}})\\
\end{bmatrix}.

Noticing that

(B\Sigma B^{T})_{j^{*},j^{*}}=1-\frac{b^{2}_{j^{*}}}{\|b\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}
,
we obtain the result.
∎

Lemma D.8
.

Consider the setting of
\mathtt{MSLR}
as in Definition
1.1
. Then
y
and
z
are independent.

Proof.

Let
g\mathbin{\overset{}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
, and
A
an event in the sigma algebra. By Bayes rule, for every
i\in[n]
it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[z_{i}=1\middle|y_{i}\in A\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{P}\left[y_{i}\in A\middle|z_{i}=1\right]\mathbb{P}[z_{i}=1]}{\mathbb{P}[y]}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{P}\left[y_{i}\in A\middle|z_{i}=1\right]\mathbb{P}[z_{i}=1]}{\mathbb{P}\left[y_{i}\in A\middle|z_{i}=1\right]\mathbb{P}[z_{i}=1]+\mathbb{P}\left[y_{i}\in A\middle|z_{i}=0\right]\mathbb{P}[z_{i}=0]}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{P}[\sqrt{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}g\in A]\phi}{\mathbb{P}[\sqrt{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}g\in A]\phi+\mathbb{P}[\sqrt{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}g\in A](1-\phi)}

\displaystyle=\phi

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}[z_{i}=1],

and the analogous result holds for the case
z_{i}=0
. The result then follows by recalling that
y_{i}
and
z_{i}
are i.i.d. across the
i
indices.
∎

High-probability events and Concentration inequalities

Lemma D.9

(High-probability events)
.

Consider the setting of
\mathtt{MSLR}
. Let
g_{q}\mathbin{\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
for
q\in[p]
and
\delta\in(0,1)
,
t>0,j^{*}\in[p]
. Define the following events that will be necessary for the analysis of
\mathtt{CORR}
on
\mathtt{MSLR}
:

\Omega_{1}(\delta):=\left\{(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})n(1-\delta)\leq\|{\bm{y}}\|^{2}\leq(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})n(1+\delta)\right\},

\Omega_{2}(t):=\left\{\max_{q\in[p]}|g_{q}|\leq\sqrt{2\log{2p}}+\sqrt{2t\log{2p}}\right\},

\displaystyle\Omega_{3}(\delta):=\left\{\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}(z_{i}{\bm{\beta}}_{1,j^{*}}+(1-z_{i}){\bm{\beta}}_{2,j^{*}})\frac{y^{2}_{i}/\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right.\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\left.\left.-(\phi{\bm{\beta}}_{1,j^{*}}+(1-\phi){\bm{\beta}}_{2,j^{*}})\frac{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right|<\sqrt{\frac{2(3+\delta)\log{(2p)}}{(1-\delta)(1+\sigma^{2}/\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2})}}\right\},

\Omega_{4}(\delta):=\left\{\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}z_{i}{\bm{\beta}}_{1,j^{*}}\frac{y^{2}_{i}/\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}-\phi{\bm{\beta}}_{1,j^{*}}\frac{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right|<\sqrt{\frac{2(3+\delta)\log{(2p)}}{(1-\delta)(1+\sigma^{2}/\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2})}}\right\},

\Omega_{5}(\delta):=\left\{\left|\|{\bm{y}}\|^{4}_{4}-3n(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})^{2}\right|<\delta n(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})^{2}\right\}.

Then, the above events all occur with high probability. Specifically,

•

\mathbb{P}\left[\Omega^{\complement}_{1}(\delta)\right]\leq 2e^{-\frac{\delta^{2}n}{8}}
as per Example 2.11 in
Wainwright 2019
noting that
y_{i}\mathbin{\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})
both marginally and conditionally on z (similarly for the setting of
\mathtt{SLR}
).

•

\mathbb{P}\left[\Omega^{\complement}_{2}(t)\right]\leq\frac{1}{(2p)^{t}}
as per Lemma 5.2 in
van Handel 2014
.

•

\mathbb{P}\left[\Omega^{\complement}_{3}(\delta)\middle|\Omega_{1}(\delta),\Omega_{5}(\delta)\right]\leq\frac{1}{2p}
as per Lemma
D.11
and consequently
\mathbb{P}\left[\Omega^{\complement}_{3}(\delta)\right]\leq\frac{1}{2p}+2e^{-\frac{\delta^{2}n}{8}}+\frac{39}{\delta^{2}n}
.

•

\mathbb{P}\left[\Omega^{\complement}_{4}(\delta)\middle|\Omega_{1}(\delta),\Omega_{5}(\delta)\right]\leq\frac{1}{2p}
as per Lemma
D.11
with
{\bm{\beta}}_{2,j^{*}}=0
and consequently
\mathbb{P}\left[\Omega^{\complement}_{4}(\delta)\right]\leq\frac{1}{2p}+2e^{-\frac{\delta^{2}n}{8}}+\frac{39}{\delta^{2}n}
.

•

\mathbb{P}\left[\Omega^{\complement}_{5}(\delta)\right]\leq\frac{39}{\delta^{2}n}
as per Lemma
D.10
.

Lemma D.10

(Fast
\ell_{4}
norm concentration)
.

Let
{\bm{y}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
with
y_{i}\mathbin{\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma^{2})
for
i\in[n]
. Then for
\delta>0
we have,

\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\|{\bm{y}}\|_{4}-3n(\sigma^{2})^{2}\right|\geq\delta n(\sigma^{2})^{2}\right]\leq\frac{39}{\delta^{2}n}.

Proof.

The proof follows from a standard application of Chebyshev’s inequality. We first recall that the
p^{\text{th}}
centered Gaussian moment is given by
(\text{variance})^{\frac{p}{2}}(p-1)!!
, and consequently by independence we have that
\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{y}}\|^{4}_{4}\right]=3n(\sigma^{2})^{2}
. We then proceed with Chebyshev’s inequality (see
Boucheron et al. 2013
) for
t>0
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\|{\bm{y}}\|^{4}_{4}-\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{y}}\|^{4}_{4}\right]\right|\geq t\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{n\text{Var}(y^{4}_{1})}{t^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{n\left(\mathbb{E}y^{8}_{1}-\left(\mathbb{E}y^{4}_{1}\right)^{2}\right)}{t^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{n(\sigma^{2})^{4}((7-1)!!-9)}{t^{2}}=\frac{39n(\sigma^{2})^{4}}{t^{2}}.

We set
t=n(\sigma^{2})^{2}
to obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\|{\bm{y}}\|^{4}_{4}-\mathbb{E}\left[\|{\bm{y}}\|^{4}_{4}\right]\right|\geq n(\sigma^{2})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{39n(k+\sigma^{2})^{4}}{n^{2}(\sigma^{2})^{4}}=\frac{39}{n}.

∎

Lemma D.11
.

Consider the setting of
\mathtt{MSLR}
. For
t>0,\delta>0,j^{*}\in[p]
we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}(z_{i}{\bm{\beta}}_{1,j^{*}}+(1-z_{i}){\bm{\beta}}_{2,j^{*}})\frac{y^{2}_{i}/\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}-(\phi{\bm{\beta}}_{1,j^{*}}+(1-\phi){\bm{\beta}}_{2,j^{*}})\frac{\|{\bm{y}}\|^{2}_{2}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right|\geq t\middle|\Omega_{1}(\delta),\Omega_{5}(\delta)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\frac{(1-\delta)}{2(3+\delta)}(1+\sigma^{2}/\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2})t^{2}\right)

Proof.

Consider
y\in\Omega_{1}(\delta)\cap\Omega_{5}(\delta)
in Lemma
D.9
. We first apply Hoeffding’s inequality (see
Boucheron et al. 2013
), then the definitions of
\Omega_{1}(\delta)
and
\Omega_{5}(\delta)
to obtain,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}(z_{i}{\bm{\beta}}_{1,j^{*}}+(1-z_{i}){\bm{\beta}}_{2,j^{*}})\frac{y^{2}_{i}/\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}-(\phi{\bm{\beta}}_{1,j^{*}}+(1-\phi){\bm{\beta}}_{2,j^{*}})\frac{\|{\bm{y}}\|^{2}_{2}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right|\geq t\middle|{\bm{y}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\frac{2t^{2}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}({\bm{\beta}}_{1,j^{*}}-{\bm{\beta}}_{2,j^{*}})^{2}\frac{y^{4}_{i}/\|{\bm{y}}\|^{2}_{2}}{(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})^{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\frac{2t^{2}}{({\bm{\beta}}_{1,j^{*}}-{\bm{\beta}}_{2,j^{*}})^{2}\frac{\|{\bm{y}}\|^{4}_{4}}{\|{\bm{y}}\|^{2}_{2}}\frac{1}{(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})^{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\frac{2t^{2}}{({\bm{\beta}}_{1,j^{*}}-{\bm{\beta}}_{2,j^{*}})^{2}\frac{3n(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})^{2}+\delta n(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})^{2}}{\|{\bm{y}}\|^{2}_{2}}\frac{1}{(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})^{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\frac{2t^{2}}{({\bm{\beta}}_{1,j^{*}}-{\bm{\beta}}_{2,j^{*}})^{2}\frac{3n(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})^{2}+\delta n(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})^{2}}{n(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})(1-\delta)}\frac{1}{(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})^{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\frac{2(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})t^{2}}{({\bm{\beta}}_{1,j^{*}}-{\bm{\beta}}_{2,j^{*}})^{2}\frac{3+\delta}{1-\delta}}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{i)}{\leq}\exp\left(-\frac{2(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})t^{2}}{4\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}\frac{3+\delta}{1-\delta}}\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\frac{(1-\delta)}{2(3+\delta)}(1+\sigma^{2}/\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2})t^{2}\right),

where
i)
follows from
({\bm{\beta}}_{1,j^{*}}-{\bm{\beta}}_{2,j^{*}})^{2}\leq\|{\bm{\beta}}_{1}-{\bm{\beta}}_{2}\|^{2}_{2}
and the triangle inequality. Applying the law of total probability to the above, we obtain the result.
∎

D.2
\mathtt{CORR}
for
\mathtt{MSLR-D}

Proof of Theorem
2.5
.

We first note that, since
({\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2})\mathbin{\overset{}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{P}_{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|_{2}}(\mathcal{D})
, the two signals have equal norm. Hence, the complement of the
\mathtt{SB}
-
\mathtt{MSLR}
regime can be equivalently expressed through the condition
\phi{\bm{\beta}}_{1}+(1-\phi){\bm{\beta}}_{2}\neq 0
.

Let
\mathtt{CORR}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})
denote the output of running
\mathtt{CORR}
on inputs
{\bm{X}},{\bm{y}}
. Consider the test function

g\left({\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{X}}\\
{\bm{y}}\end{bmatrix}}\right):=\begin{cases}\mathtt{p}&\mathtt{CORR}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})\neq\emptyset\\
\mathtt{q}&\mathtt{CORR}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})=\emptyset\end{cases}.

Let
{\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{X}}\\
{\bm{y}}\end{bmatrix}}\mathbin{\overset{}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}{{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}})\otimes{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{y}})}
. Recall that
\mathtt{CORR}
outputs the following set

\mathtt{CORR}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})=\left\{j\in[p]:\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{j},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|\geq\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}\right\}.

As in the proof of Theorem
D.1
, we note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\max_{q\in[p]}\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{q},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|\geq\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\max_{q\in[p]}\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{q},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|\geq\sqrt{2\log{2p}}+\frac{\epsilon}{2\sqrt{8}}\sqrt{\log{2p}}\right].

(26)

Noting that for
{\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{X}}\\
{\bm{y}}\end{bmatrix}}\mathbin{\overset{}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}{{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}})\otimes{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{y}})}
we have that
\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{q},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\mathbin{\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
, we apply Lemma
D.9
to (
26
) and obtain that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\max_{q\in[p]}\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{j},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|\geq\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}\right]\leq(2p)^{-\frac{1}{8}\left(\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)^{2}},

and hence we have that, under
{{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}})\otimes{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{y}})}
,
g\left({\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{X}}\\
{\bm{y}}\end{bmatrix}}\right)=\mathtt{q}
with probability
1-o(1)
.

Conversely, let
{\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{X}}\\
{\bm{y}}\end{bmatrix}}\mathbin{\overset{}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}{{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})}
. Let
J\subseteq{\rm supp}({\bm{\beta}}_{1})\cup{\rm supp}({\bm{\beta}}_{2})
such that
\phi{\bm{\beta}}_{1,j}+(1-\phi){\bm{\beta}}_{2,j}\neq 0
for
j\in J
. From Lemma
D.3
, we then have that,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[|\mathtt{CORR}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})|=0\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[\cap_{j\in[p]}\left\{\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{j},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|\leq\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\cap_{j\in J}\left\{\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{j},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|\leq\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{J_{1}},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|\leq\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{1}{p}+6e^{\frac{\delta^{2}n}{w}}+\frac{39}{\delta^{2}n}\right)=o(1),

and hence we have that, under
{{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})}
,
g\left({\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{X}}\\
{\bm{y}}\end{bmatrix}}\right)=\mathtt{p}
with probability
1-o(1)
.
∎

D.3
Recovery algorithms for
\mathtt{MSLR}

General recovery algorithm for
\mathtt{MSLR}

A recovery algorithm for
\mathtt{MSLR}
in the noiseless and balanced regimes is given in Theorem
2.6
. We measure the recovery error in
\mathtt{MSLR}
as in
Chen et al. 2014
,

\rho(\hat{\bm{\theta}},\bm{\theta}):=\min\left\{\left\|\hat{\bm{{\bm{\beta}}}}_{1}-\bm{{\bm{\beta}}}_{1}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\hat{\bm{{\bm{\beta}}}}_{2}-\bm{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2}\right\|_{2},\left\|\hat{\bm{{\bm{\beta}}}}_{1}-\bm{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\hat{\bm{{\bm{\beta}}}}_{2}-\bm{{\bm{\beta}}}_{1}\right\|_{2}\right\},

where
\hat{\bm{\theta}}=(\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{1},\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2})
and
\bm{\theta}=({\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2})
.

Proof of Theorem
2.6
.

In the case of
\sigma=0,\phi\neq 1/2
(noiseless), we first apply
\mathtt{CORR}
and then the Alternating Minimization (AM) algorithm of
Yi et al. 2014
. Theorem
D.12
shows that this succeeds in the regime of interest.

In the case of
\phi=1/2
(balanced),
\texttt{SNR}=\Omega(k)
, we first apply
\mathtt{CORR}
and then the algorithm of
Chen et al. 2014
. Theorem
D.20
proves that in the high
SNR
regime
\texttt{SNR}=\Omega(1)
, we have that
\rho(\hat{\theta},\theta)=\Theta\left(\sigma\sqrt{\frac{k}{n}}\right)
. In order for exact recovery to be achieved for all allowable finite
n
, we would require
\sqrt{\frac{\sigma^{2}k}{(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})\log{p}}}\to 0
, which is satisfied for
\texttt{SNR}=\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}/\sigma^{2}=\Omega(k)
, implying a non-vanishing signal-to-noise ratio with respect to the support set. This is satisfied by hypothesis, and hence
\mathtt{CORR}
together with Algorithm
6
succeeds in solving asymptotically exact recovery (up to relabeling of
{\bm{\beta}}_{1}
and
{\bm{\beta}}_{2}
) in the regime of interest.
∎

Recovery algorithm for noiseless, unbalanced
\mathtt{MSLR}

In this section we will show that the
\mathtt{CORR}
algorithm can be used to reduce the
\mathtt{MSLR}
problem to a dense problem where
n,k=\Theta(p)
and the signal is not assumed to be sublinearly sparse, where state-of-the-art algorithms for this dense mixed linear regression case can then infer
{\bm{\beta}}_{1}
from
{\bm{\beta}}_{2}
. In the noiseless case with
\phi\neq 1/2
, we can apply
\mathtt{CORR}
together with the existing polynomial-time Alternating Minimization (AM) algorithm from
Yi et al. 2014
to fully solve
\mathtt{MSLR}
in what is a constant number of steps. We recall that Theorem
D.1
only provided guarantees on the support recovery of the joint signal, whereas now with the execution of the AM algorithm on the reduced joint support set one can fully infer
{\bm{\beta}}_{1}
from
{\bm{\beta}}_{2}
. Define the mixture proportions,

\displaystyle\frac{n_{1}}{n}:=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}z_{i}}{n}

\displaystyle\frac{n_{2}}{n}:=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-z_{i})}{n}.

We begin by stating the theorem.

Theorem D.12

(Success of
\mathtt{CORR}+
AM on noiseless
\mathtt{MSLR}
)
.

Consider the general setting of
\mathtt{MSLR}
with parameters
p,n,k,\sigma=0
,
\phi\neq 1/2
,
({\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2})\mathbin{\overset{}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{P}_{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|_{2}}(\mathcal{D})
. Suppose

n\geq\frac{32}{\min\{\phi^{2}{\bm{\beta}}^{2}_{\texttt{min}},(1-\phi)^{2}{\bm{\beta}}^{2}_{\texttt{min}},\langle{\bm{\beta}}\rangle^{2}_{\texttt{min}}\}}(1+\epsilon)\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}\log{2p}

for
\epsilon\in(0,1)
used in
\mathtt{CORR}
. Then with probability at least
1-c_{1}(\frac{k}{p}+ke^{-c_{2}n}+\frac{k}{n}+\frac{1}{p^{c_{2}}})
for constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
, the output
\hat{\theta}=(\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{1},\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2})
of
\mathtt{CORR}+
Algorithm
3

+
Algorithm
5
satisfies

\rho(\hat{\theta},\theta)=0.

Proof of Theorem
D.12
.

By Theorem
D.1
, we can recover the joint support set (at most of size
2k
) of signals
({\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2})
with probability at least
1-c_{1}(\frac{k}{p}+ke^{-c_{2}n}+\frac{k}{n}+\frac{1}{p^{c_{2}}})
by running
\mathtt{CORR}
.

After running
\mathtt{CORR}
and identifying the
<2k
joint support set indices, we restrict the regression problem to these indices by removing all other columns from the design matrix
{\bm{X}}
(as these do not influence the output
y
since they do not correspond to support indices of
{\bm{\beta}}_{1}
or
{\bm{\beta}}_{2}
). We are then tasked with solving a two-component mixtures of regressions problem with
n
samples and signals of dimension between
k
and
2k
(importantly, the dimension is no longer
p
).

From this point, the idea is to spectrally initialize
({\bm{\beta}}_{1}^{(0)},{\bm{\beta}}^{(0)}_{2})
using Algorithm
3
for which Proposition
D.14
provides guarantees, pass
({\bm{\beta}}_{1}^{(0)},{\bm{\beta}}^{(0)}_{2})
into Algorithm
5
for which Theorem
D.15
provides guarantees on geometric error decay given this initialization, and run Algorithm
5
for a finite number of iterations guaranteed by Proposition
D.16
.

The condition on sample size
n
of Proposition
D.14
is met, since it is assumed that
n\gtrsim\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}\log{2p}\geq{\bm{\beta}}_{\texttt{min}}^{2}k\log{2p}
and
k\log{2p}=\omega(k\log^{2}{k})
. The condition of Theorem
D.15
is met by the result of Proposition
D.14
. The condition of Proposition
D.16
is met by running Algorithm
5
(resampling) with
O(k\log^{2}{k})
samples (see Remark
D.17
).

What remains to show is that
n_{1}\neq n_{2}
, as this is required by Remark
D.13
. Recall
n_{1}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}z_{i}
and
n_{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-z_{i})
and
z_{i}
are independent
\text{Bernoulli}(\phi)
. Without loss of generality assuming
\phi<1/2
, there exists a
\delta\in(0,1)
such that,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}z_{i}\geq 1/2\right]\leq{\mathbb{P}}\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}z_{i}\geq(1+\delta)\phi\right]\leq e^{-\Theta{(n)}},

after applying a standard Chernoff bound. This high probability statement can be absorbed into the
1-c_{1}(\frac{k}{p}+ke^{-c_{2}n}+\frac{k}{n}+\frac{1}{p^{c_{2}}})
high probability statement provided by
\mathtt{CORR}
, choosing adjusted constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
.

We hence conclude that running
\mathtt{CORR}
followed by Algorithm
3
followed by Algorithm
5
we obtain
\rho(\mathbf{\theta}^{(t)},\mathbf{\theta})=0
in finite
t
with probability at least
1-c_{1}(\frac{k}{p}+ke^{-c_{2}n}+\frac{k}{n}+\frac{1}{p^{c_{2}}})
.
∎

Their initialization algorithm is based on the positive semidefinite matrix:

\displaystyle M:=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}y_{i}^{2}{\bm{x}}_{i}\otimes{\bm{x}}_{i}

which serves as an unbiased estimator of a matrix whose two largest eigenvectors span the space spanned by
{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}
.

Remark D.13
.

It is stated in
Fan et al. 2018
that, when the mixture frequencies are equal to each other (
n_{1}=n_{2}
), the top two eigenvectors of
{\mathop{\mathbb{E}}}M
will not be
{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}
. Hence, their algorithms only work for the case
\phi\neq 1/2
and
\sigma=0
(noiseless).

Outside of the case
\phi=1/2
, when the mixture proportions are known, an approximation of
{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}
can be computed in closed form through Algorithm
3
, where

sign(b)=\begin{cases}1,\;b=1\\
-1,\;b=2.\end{cases}

;

Data:

Input:
n_{1},n_{2}
, samples
\{(y_{i},{\bm{x}}_{i}),i=1,2,...,n\}

;

M\leftarrow\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}y_{i}^{2}{\bm{x}}_{i}\otimes{\bm{x}}_{i}
;

Compute top 2 eigenvectors and eigenvalues
(v_{b},\lambda_{b}),b=1,2
of
(M-I)/2
;

Compute
{\bm{\beta}}_{b}^{(0)}=\sqrt{\frac{1-\Delta_{b}}{2}}v_{b}+sign(b)\sqrt{\frac{1+\Delta_{b}}{2}}v_{-b}
, where
\Delta_{b}=\frac{(\lambda_{b}-\lambda_{-b})^{2}+n_{b}^{2}-n_{-b}^{2}}{2(\lambda_{-b}-\lambda_{b})n_{b}},\;b=1,2

return

{\bm{\beta}}_{1}^{(0)},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}^{(0)}

Algorithm 3

Initialization with proportion information

In what follows, we state the iterative algorithms proposed in
Fan et al. 2018
and their guarantees.

Data:

Initial
{\bm{\beta}}_{1}^{(0)},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}^{(0)}
, # iterations
t_{0}
, samples
\{(y_{i},{\bm{x}}_{i}),i=1,2,...,N\}

for

t=0,\cdots,t_{0}-1

do

J_{1},J_{2}\leftarrow\emptyset
;

for

i=1,2,\cdots,N

do

if

\left|y_{i}-\langle{{\bm{x}}_{i}},{{\bm{\beta}}}_{1}^{(t)}\rangle\right|<\left|y_{i}-\langle{{\bm{x}}_{i}},{{\bm{\beta}}_{2}^{(t)}}\rangle\right|

then

J_{1}\leftarrow J_{1}\cup\{i\}
;

else

J_{2}\leftarrow J_{2}\cup\{i\}
;

end if

end for

;

{\bm{\beta}}_{1}^{(t+1)}\leftarrow\argmin_{{\bm{\beta}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k}}\|{{\bm{y}}_{J_{1}}-{\bm{X}}_{J_{1}}{\bm{\beta}}}\|_{2}

{\bm{\beta}}_{2}^{(t+1)}\leftarrow\argmin_{{\bm{\beta}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{k}}\|{\bm{y}}_{J_{2}}-{\bm{X}}_{J_{2}}{\bm{\beta}}\|_{2}
;

end for

return

{\bm{\beta}}_{1}^{(t_{0})},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}^{(t_{0})}

Algorithm 4

AM

Proposition D.14
.

(
Yi et al. 2014
)

Consider the initialization method in Algorithm
3
. Given any constant
\widehat{c}<1/2
, with probablity at least
1-\frac{1}{p^{2}}
, the approach produces an initialization
({\bm{\beta}}_{1}^{(0)},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}^{(0)})
satisfying

\displaystyle\rho({\mathbf{\theta}}^{(0)},\mathbf{\theta})~\leq~\widehat{c}\,\min\{n_{1}/n,n_{2}/n\}\,\|{\bm{\beta}}_{1}-{\bm{\beta}}_{2}\|_{2},

if

n\geq c_{1}\left(\frac{1}{\widetilde{\delta}}\right)^{2}\,p\,\log^{2}p.

Here
c_{1}
is a constant that depends on
\widehat{c}
. And

\sqrt{\widetilde{\delta}}=\widehat{c}\sqrt{\min\{n_{1}/n,n_{2}/n\}}^{3}\|{\bm{\beta}}_{1}-{\bm{\beta}}_{2}\|_{2}(\sqrt{1-\kappa})\kappa,

where
\kappa=\sqrt{1-4(1-\langle{{\bm{\beta}}_{1}},{{\bm{\beta}}_{2}}\rangle^{2})\frac{n_{1}}{n}\frac{n_{2}}{n}}
.

Data:

Initial
{\bm{\beta}}_{1}^{(0)},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}^{(0)}
, # iterations
t_{0}
, samples
\{(y_{i},{\bm{x}}_{i}),i=1,2,...,N\}

Partition the samples
\{(y_{i},{\bm{x}}_{i})\}
into
t_{0}
disjoint sets:
\mathcal{S}_{1},...,\mathcal{S}_{t_{0}}

for

t=1,\cdots,t_{0}

do

Use
\mathcal{S}_{t}
to run Algorithm
4
initialized with
({\bm{\beta}}_{1}^{(t-1)},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}^{(t-1)})
and returning
({\bm{\beta}}_{1}^{t},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}^{t})

end for

return

{\bm{\beta}}_{1}^{(t_{0})},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}^{(t_{0})}

Algorithm 5

AM with resampling

Theorem D.15
.

Yi et al. 2014

Consider one iteration in Algorithm
5
. For fixed
({\bm{\beta}}_{1}^{(t-1)},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}^{(t-1)})
, there exist absolute constants
\widetilde{c},c_{1},c_{2}
such that if

\rho({\mathbf{\theta}}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{\theta})~\leq~\widetilde{c}\,\min\{n_{1}/n,n_{2}/n\}\,\|\mathbf{{\bm{\beta}}}_{1}^{*}-\mathbf{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2}^{*}\|_{2},

and if the number of samples in that iteration satisfies

|\mathcal{S}_{t}|~\geq~\left(\frac{c_{1}}{\min\{n_{1}/n,n_{2}/n\}}\right)\,p,

then with probability greater than
1-\exp(-c_{2}p)
we have a geometric decrease in the error at the next stage, i.e.

\rho({\mathbf{\theta}}^{(t)},\mathbf{\theta})\leq\frac{1}{2}\rho({\mathbf{\theta}}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{\theta})

Proposition D.16
.

(Exact Recovery)
Yi et al. 2014

There exist absolute constants
c_{1},c_{2}
such that if

\rho({\mathbf{\theta}}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{\theta})\leq\frac{c_{1}}{p^{2}}\|{\bm{\beta}}_{1}-{\bm{\beta}}_{2}\|_{2}

and

\frac{1}{\min\{n_{1}/n,n_{2}/n\}}p<|\mathcal{S}_{t}|<c_{2}p,

then with probability greater than
1-\frac{1}{p}
,

\rho({\mathbf{\theta}}^{(t)},\mathbf{\theta})=0.

Remark D.17
.

It is easy to see, and remarked in
Fan et al. 2018
(pp. 11-12, above and below Proposition 4) that, running Algorithm
5
with guarantees given in Theorem
D.15
, one would require
O(p\log^{2}{p})
samples to obtain
\rho({\mathbf{\theta}}^{(t-1)},\mathbf{\theta})\leq\frac{c_{1}}{p^{2}}\|\mathbf{{\bm{\beta}}}_{1}^{*}-\mathbf{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2}^{*}\|_{2}
for the constant
c_{1}>0
suitable for Proposition
D.16
.

Algorithm for
\mathtt{MSLR}
in the balanced case

Consider the case of Mixtures of Linear Regressions (
\mathtt{MLR}
) as in
Chen et al. 2014
, where
n,k=\Theta(p)
, and
\sigma^{2}
is known. Further, consider the balanced regime where
\phi=1/2
, but
{\bm{\beta}}_{1,j}\neq-{\bm{\beta}}_{2,j}
for any
j\in\mathcal{S}_{1}\cap\mathcal{S}_{2}
(
\langle{\bm{\beta}}\rangle^{2}_{\texttt{min}}\neq 0
). We claim that we can solve the aforementioned
\mathtt{MSLR}
problem in this regime by recovering the joint support of
{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}
using
\mathtt{CORR}
, and then running the algorithm in
Chen et al. 2014
for general mixed linear regression. This latter Algorithm
6
is outlined below.

;

Data:

({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times p}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}

;

Let
(\hat{\bm{K}},\hat{\bm{g}}):=\argmin_{\bm{K},\bm{g}}\;\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(-\left\langle\bm{x}_{i}\bm{x}_{i}^{\top},\bm{K}\right\rangle+2y_{i}\left\langle\bm{x}_{i},\bm{g}\right\rangle-y_{i}^{2}+\sigma^{2}\right)^{2}+\lambda\left\|\bm{K}\right\|_{*}
;

Compute the matrix
\hat{\bm{J}}=\hat{\bm{g}}\hat{\bm{g}}^{\top}-\hat{\bm{K}}
, and its first eigenvalue-eigenvector pair
\hat{\lambda}
and
\hat{\bm{v}}
;

Compute
\hat{\bm{{\bm{\beta}}}}_{1},\hat{\bm{{\bm{\beta}}}}_{2}=\hat{\bm{g}}\pm\sqrt{\hat{\lambda}}\hat{\bm{v}}
;

return

(\hat{\bm{{\bm{\beta}}}}_{1},\hat{\bm{{\bm{\beta}}}}_{2})

Algorithm 6

Estimate
{\bm{\beta}}
’s
(
Chen et al. 2014
)

As motivated in
(
Chen et al. 2014
)
, the algorithm performs a convex penalized least squares optimization to determine matrix and vector
(\hat{\bm{K}},\hat{\bm{g}})
,
\hat{\bm{g}}
being a naive estimate of
{\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2}
and the leading eigenvector-eigenvalue of
\hat{\bm{J}}
a necessary correction. We define the value
\alpha:=\frac{\left\|\bm{{\bm{\beta}}}_{1}-\bm{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2}\right\|_{2}^{2}}{\left\|\bm{{\bm{\beta}}}_{1}\right\|_{2}^{2}+\left\|\bm{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2}\right\|_{2}^{2}}
.

Definition D.18
.

(
Chen et al. 2014
)

Let
n_{1}=\left\{i\in[n]:z_{i}=1\right\}
denote the number of samples obtained from
{\bm{\beta}}_{1}
, and
n_{2}
the analogous for
{\bm{\beta}}_{2}
. We define the following
regularity
conditions, required for our further proofs:

1.

{\bm{X}}
is an i.i.d. standard Gaussian matrix,

2.

\alpha\geq c_{3}
,

3.

\min\{n_{1},n_{2}\}\geq c_{4}p
,

4.

\lambda=\Theta(\sigma(\|{\bm{\beta}}_{1}\|_{2}+\|{\bm{\beta}}_{2}\|_{2}+\sigma)\sqrt{np}\log^{3}{n})
,

5.

n\geq c_{3}p\log^{8}{n}
,

6.

|n_{1}-n_{2}|=O\left(\sqrt{n\log{n}}\right)
,

for some constants
0<c_{3}<2
and
c_{4}
.

Theorem D.19
.

Chen et al. 2014

Suppose the conditions in Definition
D.18
hold. There exist constants
c_{1},c_{2},c_{4}>0
such that with probability at least
1-c_{1}n^{-c_{2}}
, the output
\hat{\theta}=(\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{1},\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2})
of
\mathtt{Algorithm\;3}
satisfies

\rho(\hat{\theta},\theta)\leq c_{4}\sigma\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}}\log^{4}{n}+c_{4}\min\left\{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}_{1}\|_{2}+\|{\bm{\beta}}_{2}\|_{2}},\sigma\left(\frac{p}{n}\right)^{1/4}\right\}\log^{4}{n}.

The result in Theorem
D.19
implies that, in the high-snr regime
\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}/\sigma^{2}=\Omega(1)
, we have that
\rho(\hat{\theta},\theta)=\Theta(\sigma\sqrt{\frac{p}{n}})
. This holds since
\|{\bm{\beta}}_{1}\|_{2}+\|{\bm{\beta}}_{2}\|_{2}=2\sqrt{k}
.

Theorem D.20

(Success of
\mathtt{CORR}+\mathtt{Algorithm\;\ref{algo:balanced}}
on
\mathtt{MSLR}
)
.

Consider the general setting of
\mathtt{MSLR}
with parameters
p,n,k,\phi=1/2
,
({\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2})\mathbin{\overset{}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{P}_{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|_{2}}(\mathcal{D})
. Suppose the conditions of Definition
D.18
hold. There exist constants
c_{1},c_{2},c_{4}>0
such that, provided

n\geq\frac{32}{\min\{\phi^{2}{\bm{\beta}}^{2}_{\texttt{min}},(1-\phi)^{2}{\bm{\beta}}^{2}_{\texttt{min}},\langle{\bm{\beta}}\rangle^{2}_{\texttt{min}}\}}(1+\epsilon)(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})\log{2p}

for
\epsilon\in(0,1)
used in
\mathtt{CORR}
, with probability at least
1-c_{1}(\frac{k}{p}+ke^{-c_{2}n}+\frac{k}{n}+\frac{1}{p^{c_{2}}})
, the output
\hat{\theta}=(\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{1},\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2})
of
\mathtt{CORR}+\mathtt{Algorithm\;\ref{algo:balanced}}
satisfies

\rho(\hat{\theta},\theta)\leq c_{4}\sigma\sqrt{\frac{2k}{n}}\log^{4}{n}+c_{4}\min\left\{\frac{\sigma^{2}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}_{1}\|_{2}+\|{\bm{\beta}}_{2}\|_{2}},\sigma\left(\frac{2k}{n}\right)^{1/4}\right\}\log^{4}{n}.

Proof of Theorem
D.20
.

By Theorem
D.1
, we can recover the joint support set (at most of size
2k
) of signals
({\bm{\beta}}_{1},{\bm{\beta}}_{2})
with probability at least
1-c_{1}(\frac{k}{p}+ke^{-c_{2}n}+\frac{k}{n}+\frac{1}{p^{c_{2}}})
by running
\mathtt{CORR}
.

After running
\mathtt{CORR}
and identifying the
<2k
joint support set indices, we restrict the regression problem to these indices by removing all other columns from the design matrix
{\bm{X}}
(as these do not influence the output
y
since they do not correspond to support indices of
{\bm{\beta}}_{1}
or
{\bm{\beta}}_{2}
). We are then tasked with solving a two-component mixtures of regressions problem with
n
samples and signals of dimension between
k
and
2k
. We run
\mathtt{Algorithm\;3}
on this simplified regression problem, to obtain
(\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{1},\hat{{\bm{\beta}}}_{2})
.

What remains is to show that the assumptions of Theorem
D.19
hold with
p
replaced by
2k
. Indeed, condition
1.
holds since without loss of generality we can assume
{\bm{\beta}}_{1}\neq{\bm{\beta}}_{2}
, otherwise the problem setting would be that of
\mathtt{SLR}
. Condition
3.,4.,5.
hold by the definition of the
\mathtt{MSLR}
problem, and by freedom with respect to
\lambda
and
c_{3}
.

Condition
2.
holds with probability at least
1-\exp\left(-\Theta(n)\right)
, indeed by Hoeffding’s inequality
(
Boucheron et al. 2013
)
we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[n_{1}<2c_{4}k\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}z_{i}<2c_{4}k\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}(z_{i}-\phi)<2c_{4}k-\phi n\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}(-z_{i}+\phi)\geq\phi n-2c_{4}k\right]

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\Theta(n)\right),

where
\phi n-2c_{4}k
is positive for some
c_{4}>0
since by assumption
n\geq\frac{32(1+\epsilon)}{\min\{(2\phi-1)^{2},\phi^{2},(1-\phi)^{2}\}}(1+\epsilon)(k+\sigma^{2})\log{p}
and
(k+\sigma^{2})\log{p}=\omega(k)
, analogously for
n_{2}
.

In the case
\phi=1/2
(so that
2\phi-1=0
), we have that condition
6.
holds with probability
\exp(-\Theta(\log{n}))
by Hoeffding’s inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}z_{i}-\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-z_{i})\right|\geq\Theta(\sqrt{n\log{n}})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}(2z_{i}-1)\geq\Theta(\sqrt{n\log{n}})\right]+\mathbb{P}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}(-2z_{i}+1)\geq\Theta(\sqrt{n\log{n}})\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{2\Theta(n\log{n})}{2n}\right)=\exp\left(-\Theta(\log{n})\right).

The above events occur with probability at least
1-\exp(-\Theta(\log{n}))
, and the event that
\mathtt{CORR}
succeeds occurs with probability
1-c_{1}(\frac{k}{p}+ke^{-c_{2}n}+\frac{k}{n}+\frac{1}{p^{c_{2}}})
. These two events occur together with probability at least
1-c_{1}(\frac{k}{p}+ke^{-c_{2}n}+\frac{k}{n}+\frac{1}{p^{c_{2}}})
for adjusted constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
. Applying Theorem
D.19
, we obtain the result.
∎

D.4
\mathtt{CORR}
for signed support recovery in
\mathtt{SLR}

Proof of Theorem
2.9
.

The proof proceeds similarly as that of Theorem
2.5
. Let
\mathtt{CORR}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})
denote the output of running
\mathtt{CORR}
on inputs
{\bm{X}},{\bm{y}}
. Consider the test function

g\left({\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{X}}\\
{\bm{y}}\end{bmatrix}}\right):=\begin{cases}\mathtt{p}&\mathtt{CORR}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})\neq\emptyset\\
\mathtt{q}&\mathtt{CORR}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})=\emptyset\end{cases}.

Let
{\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{X}}\\
{\bm{y}}\end{bmatrix}}\mathbin{\overset{}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}{{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}})\otimes{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{y}})}
. Recall that
\mathtt{CORR}
outputs the following set

\mathtt{CORR}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})=\left\{j\in[p]:\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{j},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|\geq\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}\right\}.

As in the proof of Theorem
2.10
, we note that for
{\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{X}}\\
{\bm{y}}\end{bmatrix}}\mathbin{\overset{}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}{{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}})\otimes{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{y}})}
we have
\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{q},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\mathbin{\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
. Applying Lemma
D.9
to (
27
) we obtain,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\max_{q\in[p]}\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{q},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|\geq\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\max_{q\in[p]}\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{q},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|\geq\sqrt{2\log{2p}}+\frac{\epsilon}{2\sqrt{8}}\sqrt{\log{2p}}\right],

(27)

\displaystyle\leq{p^{-\frac{1}{16}(\frac{\epsilon}{2})^{2}}}=o(1),

and hence we have that, under
{{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}})\otimes{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{y}})}
,
g\left({\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{X}}\\
{\bm{y}}\end{bmatrix}}\right)=\mathtt{q}
with probability
1-o(1)
.

Conversely, let
{\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{X}}\\
{\bm{y}}\end{bmatrix}}\mathbin{\overset{}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}{{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})}
. We then apply Theorem
2.10
to deduce that
\mathtt{CORR}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})={\rm supp}({\bm{\beta}})\neq\emptyset
with probability at least
1-\left(\frac{k}{p}+2ke^{-c_{2}n}+\frac{1}{p^{c_{2}}}\right)
for some constant
c_{2}>0
. Hence, under
{\mathbb{P}}({\bm{X}},{\bm{y}})
,
g\left({\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{X}}\\
{\bm{y}}\end{bmatrix}}\right)=\mathtt{p}
with probability
1-o(1)
.
∎

In what follows, we consider the modified
\mathtt{CORR}
algorithm in (
3
) for estimating the
signed
support of
{\bm{\beta}}
.

Proof of Theorem
2.10
.

Let
\mathcal{S}
denote the support set of
{\bm{\beta}}
. Define the error event

\mathcal{E}=\cup_{j\in\mathcal{S}}\left\{\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{j},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|<\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}\right\}\cup\left\{\max_{q\in\mathcal{S}^{\complement}}\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{q},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|\geq\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}\right\}.

The theorem claim follows by demonstrating that
\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{E}\right]=o(1)
. With this in mind, we perform a union bound

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{E}\right]

\displaystyle\leq k\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{j^{*}},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|<\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}\right]+\mathbb{P}\left[\max_{q\in\mathcal{S}^{\complement}}\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{q},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|\geq\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}\right]

\displaystyle:=k\nu_{1}+\nu_{2}

where
j^{*}\in\mathcal{S}
. We first focus on
\nu_{2}
, where we notice
\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{q},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\mathbin{\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
for
q\in\mathcal{S}^{\complement}
. Applying Lemma
D.9
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\nu_{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[\max_{q\in\mathcal{S}^{\complement}}\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{q},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|\geq\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\max_{q\in\mathcal{S}^{\complement}}\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{q},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|\geq\sqrt{2\log{2p}}+\frac{\epsilon}{2\sqrt{8}}\sqrt{\log{2p}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq(2p)^{-\frac{1}{16}\left(\frac{\epsilon}{2}\right)^{2}},

since
\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)}\geq\sqrt{2}+\frac{\epsilon}{2\sqrt{8}}
. Setting
n\geq\frac{8(1+\epsilon)}{{\bm{\beta}}^{2}_{\min}}(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})\log{2p}
for some
\epsilon\in(0,1)
, the bound for
\nu_{1}
follows from applying Lemma
D.21

below:

\nu_{1}\leq\frac{1}{2p}+2e^{-\frac{\delta^{2}n}{8}}.

Putting it all together, we obtain that

\mathbb{P}\left[\mathcal{E}\right]\leq\frac{k}{2p}+2ke^{-c_{2}n}+\frac{1}{p^{c_{2}}}
,
for some constant
c_{2}>0
.
∎

Lemma D.21

(Concentration bound for
\mathtt{SLR}
)
.

Consider the setting of
\mathtt{SLR}
for
j^{*}\in\mathcal{S}
. Then for
n\geq\frac{8(1+\epsilon)}{{\bm{\beta}}^{2}_{\min}}(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})\log{2p}
we have

\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{j^{*}},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|\leq\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}\right]\leq\frac{1}{p}+2e^{-\frac{\delta^{2}n}{8}}

Proof.

Let
\nu:=\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\frac{\langle{\bm{X}}_{j^{*}},{\bm{y}}\rangle}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\right|\leq\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}\right]
and
\delta>0
to be chosen later. We begin by conditioning on the event
\Omega_{1}:=\Omega_{1}(\delta)
from Lemma
D.9
, and apply Lemma
D.5
(for the case
z_{i}=1
) denoting
g_{i}\mathbin{\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\kern 0.0pt\sim}}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
for
i\in[n]
as independent (also from
y
,
z
) unit normal Gaussians, obtaining

\displaystyle\nu

\displaystyle=\int\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\frac{{\bm{X}}_{j^{*}}^{T}\xi}{\|\xi\|_{2}}\right|<\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}\;\middle|\;{\bm{y}}\right]d\mathbb{P}\left[y\right]

\displaystyle=\int\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{i}\frac{y_{i}}{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}\sqrt{1-\frac{{\bm{\beta}}^{2}_{j^{*}}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}}+\frac{{\bm{\beta}}_{j^{*}}\cdot y^{2}_{i}/\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right|<\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}\;\middle|\;{\bm{y}}\right]d\mathbb{P}\left[y\right]

\displaystyle=\int\mathbb{P}\left[\left|g_{1}\sqrt{1-\frac{{\bm{\beta}}^{2}_{j^{*}}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}}+\frac{{\bm{\beta}}_{j^{*}}\cdot\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right|<\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}\;\middle|\;{\bm{y}}\right]d\mathbb{P}\left[y\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[\left|g_{1}\sqrt{1-\frac{{\bm{\beta}}^{2}_{j^{*}}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}}+\frac{{\bm{\beta}}_{j^{*}}\cdot\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right|<\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}\;\middle|\;\Omega_{1}\right]+\mathbb{P}\left[\Omega^{\complement}_{1}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[\left\{g_{1}\sqrt{1-\frac{{\bm{\beta}}^{2}_{j^{*}}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}}+\frac{{\bm{\beta}}_{j^{*}}\cdot\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}<\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}\right\}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 50.00008pt\left.\cap\left\{-\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}\leq g_{1}\sqrt{1-\frac{{\bm{\beta}}^{2}_{j^{*}}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}}+\frac{{\bm{\beta}}_{j^{*}}\cdot\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right\}\;\middle|\;\Omega_{1}\right]+\mathbb{P}\left[\Omega^{\complement}_{1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[g_{1}\sqrt{1-\frac{{\bm{\beta}}^{2}_{j^{*}}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}}<\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}-\left|\frac{{\bm{\beta}}_{j^{*}}\cdot\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right|\;\middle|\;\Omega_{1}\right]+\mathbb{P}\left[\Omega^{\complement}_{1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[g_{1}\sqrt{1-\frac{{\bm{\beta}}^{2}_{j^{*}}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}}<\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}-\left|\frac{{\bm{\beta}}_{j^{*}}\cdot\sqrt{n(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})(1-\delta)}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right|\;\middle|\;\Omega_{1}\right]+\mathbb{P}\left[\Omega^{\complement}_{1}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left[g_{1}\sqrt{1-\frac{{\bm{\beta}}^{2}_{j^{*}}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}}<\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)\log{2p}}-\left|{\bm{\beta}}_{j^{*}}\sqrt{\frac{n(1-\delta)}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}}\right|\;\middle|\;\Omega_{1}\right]+\mathbb{P}\left[\Omega^{\complement}_{1}\right].

Now setting
n\geq\frac{8(1+\epsilon)}{{\bm{\beta}}^{2}_{\min}}(\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2})\log{2p}
and applying standard sub-Gaussian bounds (see
Wainwright 2019
) we obtain

\displaystyle\nu

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left[g\sqrt{1-\frac{{\bm{\beta}}^{2}_{j^{*}}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}}<(\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)}-\sqrt{8(1+\epsilon)(1-\delta)})\sqrt{\log{2p}}\right]+\mathbb{P}\left[\Omega_{1}^{\complement}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\frac{(\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)}-\sqrt{8(1+\epsilon)(1-\delta)})^{2}\log{2p}}{2\left(1-\frac{{\bm{\beta}}^{2}_{j^{*}}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right)}\right)+2e^{-\frac{\delta^{2}n}{8}},

where

\displaystyle\sqrt{2(1+\epsilon/2)}-\sqrt{8(1+\epsilon)(1-\delta)}<-\sqrt{2}

(28)

for
\delta>0
small enough. Hence, choosing
\delta
to satisfy (
28
) above, we obtain that for
k
large enough,

\displaystyle\nu

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\frac{2\log{2p}}{2\left(1-\frac{{\bm{\beta}}^{2}_{j^{*}}}{\|{\bm{\beta}}\|^{2}_{2}+\sigma^{2}}\right)}\right)+2e^{-\frac{\delta^{2}n}{8}}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\frac{2\log{2p}}{2}\right)+2e^{-\frac{\delta^{2}n}{8}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2p}+2e^{-\frac{\delta^{2}n}{8}}.

∎
